⏺ I read the full PDF (4 pages). This is everything it asks for, plus the parts
  that are easy to miss.

  What they're really testing

  The role is about directing AI, not writing code. They want to see you decide
  who the product is for, define what it must do, and check that what the AI
  builds is correct. The code is only one of three deliverables, and the PDF
  says the transcript "matters as much as the code." A small app that works and
  is well specified beats a big one. The time budget is 2–4 hours.

  Hard requirements for the app

  1. It runs in a current version of Chrome, Edge, Firefox or Safari.
  2. A reviewer can start it on their own machine by following the README alone,
     with no paid accounts, API keys or cloud services. A public URL is
     optional and only counts in addition to local setup, not instead of it.
  3. Any language, framework or library is fine.
  4. Every story marked Implemented must work in the app exactly as its
     acceptance criteria say.
  5. Invalid input and errors (for example, dividing by zero) must show a clear
     message. The app must never crash or show a wrong result.
  6. The type of calculator is our choice, and the README must say what we chose
     and why.

  Rules for using the AI tool

  - The AI writes the code. You explain the problem, give it context, review its
    output, test it and correct it.
  - Any code you change by hand must be listed in the README.
  - You can use several sessions or tools, but every transcript must be
    submitted.
  - AI can help write the docs, but you own them and must be able to explain any
    part.

  Deliverables

  firstname-lastname-calculator/
    README.md
    src/
    docs/
      app-roles.md
      jobs-to-be-done.md
      user-stories.md
    transcripts/
      session-01.md   (one file per session)

  1. Source code and README
  - Leave out node_modules, dist, virtual environments and other build output.
  - The README must cover:
    - what the app is and who it's for, including why we chose this calculator
    - prerequisites with versions
    - the exact commands to install and start it
    - how to run the tests
    - the AI tool and model used
    - any code changed by hand
    - assumptions

  2. Transcripts
  - Each one is the full conversation from first message to last, with your
    prompts exactly as typed.
  - Export with the tool's own feature: /export, or the logs under
    ~/.claude/projects/.
  - A summary doesn't count, including one the AI writes.
  - Remove passwords, keys and tokens, and change nothing else.

  3. Product docs, written in this order because each builds on the one before:

  Doc: App Roles
  Entry format: "A [role] is [who/situation]. They can [see/do]. They must never

  [what the app prevents]" (the last part only where it applies)
  Rules: One role or several. Each must be a real kind of person, and if there
  are several, explain how their needs differ.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Doc: Jobs To Be Done
  Entry format: "When [situation], I want to [motivation], so I can [outcome]."
  Each job names its role.
  Rules: A job can't mention the app or its features. If it needs a button or
  screen to make sense, it's a feature, not a job.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Doc: User Stories
  Entry format: "As a [role], I want [capability], so that [benefit]." Each
  names
  its job, has acceptance criteria and has a status.
  Rules: A story that serves no job doesn't belong. The chain is story → job →
  role.

  - Acceptance criteria: Given/When/Then. They must cover the normal case,
    invalid input, boundary values, very large and very small numbers, and what 
    happens when the user carries on after a result or an error.
  - Status: Implemented or Not implemented, based on what the app does today.
    Stories planned for later are welcome if marked Not implemented.

  4. Sending
  - Reply to HR now to confirm you received it and say when you'll submit. This
    counts toward the delivery score.
  - Share a link (a Google Drive folder or a public Git repo), not a zip.
  - Subject line: Take-home assignment: [role you applied for], [your full name]
  - The email must include the link, the live URL if you deploy one, the AI
    tools and models used, and roughly how long you spent.
  - Be ready for a follow-up call where you walk them through your decisions.

  How they'll review it

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Criterion  │                 What they actually check                  │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ They follow the README on a clean computer and use the    │
  │ It works    │ app the way a real user would. Tests are a bonus; they    │
  │             │ still check by hand.                                      │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Product     │ Roles are real people with different needs, jobs are free │
  │ thinking    │  of solutions, every story traces to a job, and ACs are   │
  │             │ testable and cover errors and edge cases.                 │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Working     │ From the transcript: how you framed the problem, what     │
  │ with AI     │ context you gave, how you checked the output, and which   │
  │             │ mistakes you caught.                                      │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Accuracy    │ The docs and README match what was built. An honest "Not  │
  │             │ implemented" beats an overstated claim.                   │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Delivery    │ You said when you'd submit and hit that date, and the     │
  │             │ package is easy to review.                                │
  └─────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Easy to miss

  1. "Carry on after a result or error." Most submissions skip this. For
     example: after a "Cannot divide by zero" message, what does the next key
     press do?
  2. "Never a wrong result" includes floating-point maths. In JavaScript, 0.1 + 
     0.2 gives 0.30000000000000004, large numbers lose precision, and Infinity
     or NaN can end up on screen. We need to decide how to handle these.
  3. "Exactly as described" cuts both ways. Criteria with exact message text are
     testable, but then every one has to pass. Each Implemented story should be
     checked by hand before we label it.
  4. Reconcile docs and README last. After the final build, re-check every
     status and claim.
  5. Clean-computer risk. Fewer prerequisites means less can break. One gotcha:
     JavaScript modules don't load from a local file:// path in Chrome. If we
     use them, the README needs a step that starts a local server.
  6. Hand edits. It's easiest to make none. If you do make some, note them as
     you go.
  7. This session is transcript #1. Your prompts are part of what's graded. Your
     Claude Code setup also has several plugins adding instructions to my
     context (Vercel, ponytail, superpowers, gstack). One plugin's instruction
     this session told me to start building right away; I didn't, because you
     said not to code. Whether those plugins stay on during the graded sessions
     is your call.
  8. Model to name in the README: Claude Code with Claude Opus 5.5
     (claude-opus-5-5). If you switch models, list each one.
  9. Open questions. Either ask HR or make an assumption and write it in the
     README; how you handle these is part of the review. We should keep a
     running list of assumptions.
  10. I'd keep the assignment PDF out of the package. The PDF doesn't address
      this; it's my assumption.

  Decisions needed before we start

  1. What kind of calculator, and who it's for. This is the main product
     decision and everything else follows from it.
  2. Stack. I'd go minimal: plain HTML, CSS and JavaScript, with tests using
     Node's built-in test runner. That means no dependencies to install and
     nothing much to break on a clean machine.
  3. Public URL. Optional; GitHub Pages is free if you want one.
  4. Your full name for the folder, the role you applied for for the subject
     line, and your deadline.
  5. Have you replied to HR yet?

  Want me to save this as a checklist file outside the submission folder so we
  can tick it off before sending?

❯ let's build a loan and emi calculator for people comparing personal loans.   
  define who we're helping, what decision they're making and the smallest      
  useful version. keep the scope realistic.                                    

⏺ Loan & EMI Calculator: product definition

  Target user

  Primary role: Personal Loan Shopper. This is a salaried or self-employed adult
  who needs a lump sum for a planned expense, such as medical bills, a wedding,
  a renovation or paying off credit card debt. They have two or three personal
  loan offers in hand, for example from their own bank, a fintech app and a
  pre-approved offer. Each offer is quoted differently. They aren't finance
  experts, and they will probably check the numbers on their phone just before
  deciding.

  I'd use one role. The assignment allows that, and a second role that the app
  barely serves would be padding. These are the users I considered and left out:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │              Considered              │           Why not now            │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Existing borrower deciding whether   │ The maths and the decision are   │
  │ to prepay or move their loan to      │ different. Good next phase.      │
  │ another lender                       │                                  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Loan agent or advisor showing        │ Needs branding, sharing and      │
  │ options to clients                   │ client handling. That's a        │
  │                                      │ different product.               │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Lenders                              │ Not our user. They have their    │
  │                                      │ own tools.                       │
  └──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  Their main problems

  These are written without naming any feature, so they can become the Jobs To
  Be Done.

  1. Offers can't be compared at a glance. Each one has a different rate, length
     and fee.
  2. The lowest rate isn't always the cheapest loan. A processing fee (typically
     1–3%) is usually taken out of the money they receive, so they get less
     cash and pay more overall.
  3. A smaller monthly payment can hide a bigger total bill. A longer loan feels
     cheaper but costs more in interest.
  4. They need to know the monthly payment fits their budget before they sign.
  5. They don't trust the lender's own numbers. A bank's calculator only shows
     that bank's product and often leaves the fee out.

  MVP scope

  Must have. Everything here gets fully specified, built and checked by hand:

  ┌─────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────┐
  │  #  │                    Capability                     │    Solves     │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │     │ For one offer (amount, annual interest rate,      │               │
  │ M1  │ length in months or years), show the EMI (the     │ 4             │
  │     │ fixed monthly payment), total interest and total  │               │
  │     │ amount repaid                                     │               │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │     │ A processing fee entered as a % or a fixed        │               │
  │ M2  │ amount. Show the fee, the cash actually received, │ 2, 5          │
  │     │  and the total cost of borrowing (interest plus   │               │
  │     │ fee).                                             │               │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │     │ Compare up to 3 offers side by side. Label        │               │
  │ M3  │ "Lowest total cost" and "Lowest monthly payment"  │ 1, 2, 3       │
  │     │ separately, and show how much more each other     │               │
  │     │ offer costs.                                      │               │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │     │ Validation: a clear inline message for each bad   │ Assignment    │
  │ M4  │ field. Results are hidden while any input is      │ requirement   │
  │     │ invalid and come back once it's fixed.            │               │
  └─────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────┘

  The edge cases M4 must handle:
  - 0% interest: the standard formula divides by zero here, so this case needs
    its own calculation (EMI = amount ÷ months).
  - Fee too large: a fee equal to or larger than the loan amount is rejected.
  - Out-of-range values: empty, negative or out-of-range values are rejected,
    and very large amounts are capped.

  Should have, only if the Must list is done and verified:
  - S1, flat-rate offers. Some lenders quote a "flat" rate, which charges
    interest on the full amount for the whole loan even as you pay it down. A
    12% flat rate costs much more than 12% calculated the standard way, and
    putting both through the same comparison exposes that. It's a small formula
    and a real trap for borrowers.
  - S2, month-by-month schedule for one offer, showing principal, interest and
    remaining balance.

  What makes it feel like a real product, without adding scope:
  - Money shown with thousands separators and 2 decimal places, using clear
    labels like "Monthly payment (EMI)".
  - One-line plain-language hints, for example "Processing fee is usually
    deducted from the amount you receive."
  - Results update as you type, so there's no Calculate button.
  - Layout works on a phone first.
  - Basic accessibility: every field has a label, it works with a keyboard, and
    errors are announced to screen readers.
  - A short disclaimer: "Estimates only. Your lender's figures may differ
    slightly due to rounding."

  Success test: someone with 3 offers in hand can enter them and see which one
  costs least overall, and by how much, in under a minute.

  What we leave out

  ┌────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┐
  │             Leave out              │               Reason               │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Live lender rates, marketplace,    │ Needs outside APIs and cloud       │
  │ "apply now"                        │ services, which the assignment     │
  │                                    │ forbids. Also regulated.           │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Accounts, login, saving to a       │ No backend means nothing to        │
  │ server                             │ install and no personal data to    │
  │                                    │ protect                            │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Credit score or eligibility checks │ Needs credit bureau access and     │
  │                                    │ personal data. Regulated.          │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ "You should take Offer B"          │ That's financial advice. We show   │
  │ recommendations                    │ facts and labels, not verdicts.    │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ APR (true annual cost including    │ Genuinely useful, but it needs an  │
  │ fees)                              │ iterative solver. Next phase,      │
  │                                    │ marked Not implemented.            │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Prepayment, rates that change over │ Different maths. Each would be its │
  │  time, payment holidays, rising    │  own feature.                      │
  │ payments                           │                                    │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Currency conversion, taxes on fees │ Varies by region. Users can        │
  │                                    │ include tax in the fee they enter. │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Mortgages, car loans with a large  │ Different mechanics. We're focused │
  │ final payment, buy-now-pay-later   │  on personal loans.                │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Charts, dark mode, PDF/CSV export, │ Polish that doesn't help the       │
  │  share links                       │ comparison. Later, if ever.        │
  └────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘

  Assumptions to record in the README

  - Fixed rate, monthly payments, standard EMI formula: P·r·(1+r)ⁿ / ((1+r)ⁿ
    − 1), where r is the annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100.
  - The fee is charged once, upfront, and taken out of the money the borrower
    receives.
  - No currency conversion: all amounts are in whatever currency the user
    borrows in.
  - Proposed input limits:
    - amount: 1 to 100,000,000
    - rate: 0 to 50%
    - length: 1 to 120 months
    - fee: 0 to 10%, or less than the loan amount if entered as a fixed sum

  Your call

  1. Currency display. "EMI" is mostly a South Asian term, while the reviewers
     are at KnowledgeCity, which is probably US-based. I'd recommend no currency
     symbol, with the label "Monthly payment (EMI)" so it reads clearly
     anywhere. The alternative is ₹ with Indian digit grouping (1,00,000), which
     looks more realistic but is harder for reviewers to read.
  2. Should-have items. Are S1 (flat rate) and S2 (schedule) in, as stretch
     goals, or out?
  3. Input limits. Are the proposed limits above OK? They become exact numbers
     in the acceptance criteria, so they need to be settled before the docs.

  Once those are settled, I'll write docs/app-roles.md, then the jobs, then the
  user stories, before any code.

⏺ I've written two files and no code yet.

  - README.md: your decisions, the MVP scope (including what's left for later
    and what's not planned), a table of terms, and the assumptions. The sections
    for setup, run commands and tests are marked "to be written once the app is
    built".
  - docs/app-roles.md: one role, the Personal Loan Shopper, written in the
    assignment's exact "A … is … They can … They must never …" format. Below it
    is a short section explaining why there's only one role and who else I
    considered.

  Your decisions as recorded:
  - Amounts show no currency symbol, and the payment is labelled "Monthly
    payment (EMI)".
  - Flat-rate offers and the month-by-month schedule are listed under "Later".
  - Loan length must be a whole number (1–120 months or 1–10 years). A fixed fee
    must be less than the amount borrowed.
  - The upfront fee doesn't change the EMI. The EMI is always calculated on the
    amount borrowed. "Amount borrowed" and "Amount received" are separate labels
    used everywhere.

  Assumptions I added that you didn't specify. Change any of these before I 
  write the user stories, because the acceptance criteria will depend on them:
  1. Rounding: the EMI is rounded to 0.01 before totals are calculated, so the
     EMI shown times the number of months always equals the total repaid shown.
     Without this, a reviewer checking by hand could find a gap of a few cents
     and count it as a wrong result.
  2. Different amounts: the "Lowest total cost" and "Lowest monthly payment"
     labels only appear when all offers are for the same amount borrowed. A
     smaller loan always looks cheaper, so ranking offers for different amounts
     would mislead. If amounts differ, a message explains why there are no
     labels.
  3. One bad offer doesn't block the rest: an offer with an invalid field shows
     no results and is left out of the comparison, but the other offers still
     work.
  4. Number entry: commas typed into numbers are ignored, so 5,00,000 works. Up
     to 2 decimal places are allowed.
  5. Number display: numbers always appear as 1,234,567.89, whatever the
     browser's language, so the acceptance criteria give the same result for
     every reviewer.
  6. No storage: nothing is saved, so refreshing the page clears everything.

  For now the files are at the project root. When we package, the folder gets
  renamed to firstname-lastname-calculator and the assignment PDF is left out.

  Next is docs/jobs-to-be-done.md, written without mentioning the app or its
  features.

      67  - Fixed interest rate for the whole loan, charged on the reduci
          ng balance, with one payment a month.
      68  - EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n / ((1 + r)^n − 1), where P is the amo
          unt borrowed, r is the annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100, and n is the num
          ber of months. At 0% interest, EMI = P ÷ n.
      69  - **The upfront fee does not change the EMI.** The EMI is alway
          s calculated on the full amount borrowed, not the amount receiv
          ed.
      70 -- The EMI is rounded to the nearest 0.01 (halves round up) befo
         -re any totals are calculated, so the displayed EMI times the nu
         -mber of months always equals the displayed total repaid. Real l
         -enders often adjust the final payment by a small amount, so the
         -ir totals may differ slightly.                                 
      70 +- **Rounding follows lender practice.** The EMI is rounded to t
         +he nearest 0.01 (halves round up). Each month's interest is cha
         +rged on the remaining balance and rounded to the nearest 0.01. 
         +The final payment is whatever clears the balance exactly, so it
         + can differ slightly from the EMI.                             
      71 +- Every amount shown is exact to 0.01, and the figures always a
         +dd up: total repaid = EMI × (months − 1) + final payment, and t
         +otal interest = total repaid − amount borrowed. At 0% interest,
         + total interest is exactly 0.00.                               
      72 +- We do not use total repaid = EMI × months. With a rounded EMI
         +, that formula overstates or understates the real total and bre
         +aks at the edges. For example, borrowing 100 at 0% over 3 month
         +s gives an EMI of 33.33, and 33.33 × 3 = 99.99, which shows −0.
         +01 interest on an interest-free loan.                          
      73  - A percentage fee is calculated on the amount borrowed and rou
          nded to the nearest 0.01.
      74 +- Lenders round in different ways (some to whole units), so the
         +ir figures may differ from ours by a small amount. For typical 
         +offers (10–36% interest, 12–60 months) the final payment is wit
         +hin 1.00 of the EMI.

⏺ I checked the rounding rule I wrote last time by simulating it, and it gave
  wrong results. The README now uses a different method and the comparison
  rules, and docs/jobs-to-be-done.md is written. No app code yet.

  Rounding

  The previous rule (total repaid = EMI × months) gives misleading results:

  ┌───────────────────────┬─────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
  │         Loan          │  EMI × months   │       Shown as interest       │
  ├───────────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ 100 at 0% over 3      │ 33.33 × 3 =     │ −0.01 on an interest-free     │
  │ months                │ 99.99           │ loan                          │
  ├───────────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ 1,000 at 0% over 120  │ 8.33 × 120 =    │ −0.40                         │
  │ months                │ 999.60          │                               │
  ├───────────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ 1 at 0% over 120      │ 0.01 × 120 =    │ 0.20 of interest that doesn't │
  │ months                │ 1.20            │  exist                        │
  └───────────────────────┴─────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘

  What I changed it to: the method lenders actually use.
  - The EMI is rounded to 0.01.
  - Each month's interest is rounded to 0.01.
  - The final payment is whatever clears the remaining balance, so it can be
    slightly different from the EMI.

  With this method every figure on screen adds up: EMI × (months − 1) + final
  payment = total repaid. Interest at 0% is exactly 0.00. The final payment only
  appears when it differs from the EMI. In a 20,000-case sample of typical
  offers, it was never more than 0.84 away from the EMI.

  ⚠️ One change to a limit you approved: the minimum amount borrowed goes from 1
  to 1,000. With very small loans, the rounded EMI on a long, high-rate loan
  pays the loan off before the last month, and the final payment comes out
  negative. I tested every loan length and rates in 0.25% steps:
  - a 100 minimum still gives a final payment as low as −10.97
  - a 1,000 minimum leaves the smallest final payment at +7.77

  1,000 is also a realistic floor for a personal loan in most currencies. If
  you'd rather keep a lower minimum, the alternative is a separate validation
  rule for tiny payments, which adds complexity for an unrealistic case.

  Comparison rules

  ┌─────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │    Situation    │                  What the user sees                   │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ No valid offers │ No results, no labels                                 │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 1 valid offer   │ Its results and "Add another offer to compare."       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Different       │ Each offer's results, but no labels or differences,   │
  │ amounts         │ and a message asking for the same amount in every     │
  │ borrowed        │ offer                                                 │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Tie for lowest  │ Every tied offer gets the label with "(tied)" added,  │
  │                 │ and none of them shows a difference                   │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ "Lowest total cost" and "Lowest monthly payment" (one │
  │ Normal          │  offer can have both). Every other offer shows "Costs │
  │                 │  X more in total".                                    │
  └─────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  - Rankings use the rounded figures shown on screen, so a label never
    contradicts the numbers next to it.
  - An offer with every field empty doesn't count and shows no error messages.

  Jobs To Be Done

  Five jobs, all for the Personal Loan Shopper. None of them mentions the app, a
  field or a button.
  - J-1: find which offer costs the least overall.
  - J-2: know how much cash actually arrives after the fee.
  - J-3: know the monthly payment and whether it fits the budget.
  - J-4: see what a lower payment from a longer loan costs overall.
  - J-5: check a lender's figures independently.

  Each job has a one-line note on why it's a real need. One job I left out
  (paying a loan off early) is listed at the bottom with the reason: it belongs
  to the existing-borrower role we excluded.

  The throwaway simulation is at
  /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sallar-Downloads-KnowledgeCity/ea904531-f839-4c
  f2-bff2-d1f467cc45b0/scratchpad/rounding_check.py. It's outside the project
  and won't be submitted.

  Can I keep the 1,000 minimum? If so, the user stories come next, with exact
  figures in the acceptance criteria worked out ahead of time.

  stories, all marked Not implemented, and each one names the job it serves.
  Every figure in the criteria comes from the calculation script, not from me

  What's covered

  Story: S-1 Monthly payment
  Serves: J-3
  What it covers: Normal case; months vs years; 0% interest; 1-month and
  largest-allowed loans; a very low rate; commas and spaces in input; German
  browser language; phone width; keyboard-only use
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-2 Totals add up
  Serves: J-5
  What it covers: Final payment; EMI × (months − 1) + final = total, to the
  cent;
  0% gives exactly 0.00 interest; smallest and largest values; the "estimates
  only" note
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-3 Fee and amount received
  Serves: J-2
  What it covers: % and fixed fees; the fee leaves the EMI unchanged; an empty
  fee counts as 0; switching fee type; a fee just under the loan amount;
  reducing the loan below a fixed fee, then correcting it
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-4 Lowest total cost
  Serves: J-1
  What it covers: The lowest-rate offer isn't the cheapest; a "0% interest"
  offer
  whose fee makes it cost more; two-way and three-way ties; one valid offer;
  none valid; different amounts
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-5 Lowest monthly payment
  Serves: J-4
  What it covers: A longer loan has the lowest payment but the highest cost; one

  offer winning both labels; an EMI tie caused by different fees
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-6 Error messages
  Serves: J-5
  What it covers: 27 invalid inputs with their exact messages; values right at
  each limit are accepted; correcting a box; several errors fixed one at a
  time; clearing an offer; one offer's error doesn't affect the others; the
  page never crashes
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-7 Updates as you change offers
  Serves: J-1
  What it covers: Labels move to another offer when a rate changes; an offer
  turns invalid and then is fixed; amounts become different and then match
  again; going from one offer to two and back
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-8 Privacy
  Serves: J-6
  What it covers: No network requests; works offline; reloading clears
  everything; never asks for personal details
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Story: S-9 to S-11 (later)
  Serves: J-1, J-5
  What it covers: Flat rate, the month-by-month schedule and APR, each with
  concrete figures

  Changes you should check

  1. New job J-6 (privacy). The role says the Shopper's details must never leave
     their device. Without a job to serve, a story about that would break the
     assignment's rule that every story needs a job. Each job now also lists the
     stories that serve it.
  2. Interface details I had to fix so the criteria can be tested. These are
     recorded in the README and at the top of the stories file:
     - Layout: three offer panels are always on screen. There's no Calculate
       button; results update as you type.
     - When messages appear: an error message appears when you leave a box.
       After that it updates as you type, but figures disappear the moment any
       input is invalid.
     - Optional fee: the fee box can be left empty, which counts as 0.
     - Switching units: changing between months and years, or between % and a
       fixed fee, keeps the number you typed.
     - Typed input: typing 12% is rejected. Negative numbers get the range
       message rather than a format message.
     - Message wording: every message's exact text is defined in one table.
  3. README: the 1,000 minimum is now described as a product decision.

  When you walk HR through the submission, keep in mind that the transcript
  shows the 1,000 minimum came out of the rounding check, so be ready to explain
  both reasons.

  Next is choosing the tech stack and planning the build. My recommendation is
  still plain HTML, CSS and JavaScript with Node's built-in test runner, so a
  reviewer has almost nothing to install. Shall I go ahead with that?

⏺ I've updated the docs. Below is the build plan; no code has been written yet.

  Doc updates

  - S-8.1 (privacy) now checks that nothing you type is sent anywhere. The test
    value is 123,456.78 because it won't appear anywhere in our own code, so
    searching the browser's Network tab for it can't give a false match. The
    page is still allowed to load its own files. The README privacy note is
    reworded to match.
  - When error messages appear. The old README rule couldn't satisfy S-3.7:
    lowering the amount borrowed has to show a message on the fee box, which the
    user didn't just leave. The new rule:
    - When you leave any box, every box in that offer that you've already
      visited and that is invalid shows its message.
    - Switching months/years or %/fixed counts as leaving that box.

  Project structure

  (project root, renamed firstname-lastname-calculator when packaging)
  ├── README.md
  ├── docs/                    roles, jobs, stories (done)
  ├── transcripts/             session exports, added at the end
  ├── index.html · package.json · vite.config.ts · tsconfig*.json · 
  components.json
  └── src/
      ├── main.tsx             React entry point
      ├── index.css            Tailwind and the shadcn theme colours
      ├── App.tsx              the page: typed text for 3 offers → derived 
  results and comparison
      ├── App.test.tsx         a few interface tests
      ├── components/
      │   ├── OfferCard.tsx    one offer: boxes, unit selects, messages, 
  figures, labels
      │   └── ui/              shadcn-generated: card, input, label, badge
      ├── lib/utils.ts         shadcn's cn() helper (generated)
      └── domain/              plain TypeScript: no React, no browser
          ├── validation.ts    typed text → valid offer, or an error per box
          ├── loan.ts          valid offer → EMI, final payment, totals
          ├── comparison.ts    results → labels, "Costs … more", page message
          ├── format.ts        cents → "1,234,567.89"
          ├── messages.ts      every user-facing message, matching the Messages 
  table in the docs
          └── *.test.ts        one test file per module, with test names that 
  cite story IDs

  The config files have to sit at the root next to src/. That way the reviewer
  can run npm install && npm run dev from the top folder, which still matches
  the assignment's layout.

  How calculations, validation and the interface stay separate

  typed text ─► validateOffer ─► calculateLoan ─► compareOffers ─► OfferCard 
  draws it
   (strings)     domain           domain           domain           (formats at 
  the last step)

  validateOffer(input: OfferInput):
    | { status: 'empty' }
    | { status: 'invalid'; errors: Partial<Record<Field, string>> }
    | { status: 'valid'; offer: ValidOffer }

  calculateLoan(offer: ValidOffer): LoanResult                 // every amount 
  in whole cents
  compareOffers(results: (LoanResult | null)[]): Comparison    // null = offer 
  not valid
  formatMoney(cents: number): string

  - Calculations (domain/) are pure functions. They're tested without a browser
    and contain all the maths and rules.
    - Money is stored as whole cents and rates as hundredths of a percent. Text
      is converted straight to whole numbers rather than parsed as decimals, so
      problems like 0.1 + 0.2 can't happen.
    - The only floating-point step is the EMI formula, and its result is rounded
      once.
  - Validation owns the input rules and the message text. It doesn't know
    anything about focus or which boxes have been visited.
  - Interface:
    - App stores only the raw text of the three offers. Everything else is
      recalculated from that text on every render, so results can never go
      stale.
    - OfferCard stores only display state: which boxes have been visited and
      which messages are showing.
    - Numbers are formatted only at the moment they're drawn.

  Where shadcn/ui is worth it

  ✅
  Component: Card, Input, Label, Badge
  Reason: One consistent theme, focus rings and error styling, with no logic of
  ours inside
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ❌ native <select>
  Component: months/years, %/fixed
  Reason: Phones show their own picker and it's accessible by default. The Radix

  Select/ToggleGroup add JavaScript, and ToggleGroup lets a click clear the
  selection, which we'd have to guard against.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ❌ our own pure functions
  Component: Form (react-hook-form + zod)
  Reason: Our rules are specific (a fee checked against the amount, messages
  shown on leaving a box). A form library would add a layer without removing
  any code.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ❌ plain hint text
  Component: Tooltip
  Reason: Tooltips don't work on touch screens

  Testing

  - Logic tests (Vitest) cover every figure in S-1 to S-7, the S-6.1 error table
    and the S-6.2 boundary values. I'll write them from the docs before the
    logic, and each test name will cite its story ID.
  - Interface tests (Vitest + Testing Library + jsdom). A handful of flows that
    the pure tests can't see:
    - when messages appear and clear (S-6.3, 6.4, 6.6, 6.7)
    - the fee/amount check (S-3.7, 3.8)
    - labels on screen (S-4.1) and live updates (S-7.1)
  - Checks by hand in a real browser for what jsdom can't simulate: German
    browser language (S-1.10), 375px phone width (S-1.11), keyboard order
    (S-1.12), and network, offline and reload (S-8). No story gets marked
    Implemented until its checks pass.

  Problems caught along the way

  - 6 vulnerabilities (2 low, 4 moderate): the shadcn installer added its own
    command-line tool as a dependency that ships with the app, purely so one
    stylesheet import would work. None of our components used anything from that
    stylesheet, so I removed the import and the tool, and the count went to
    zero. The tool still runs through npx when we add components.
  - Unwanted extras: shadcn also added a Button component and an icon library.
    We use neither (there's no Calculate button), so both are gone.
  - Strict type checking: TypeScript 7's template doesn't set strict. I compiled
    a test file to confirm it's on by default, then set it explicitly anyway so
    a reviewer can see it in the config.
  - An unfamiliar dependency called cn: shadcn's components now import a package
    with this name. I checked before trusting it. It's published by shadcn's
    own account from the shadcn-ui/cn repository, and it replaces two older
    packages (clsx and tailwind-merge).
  - Lint warning: it's in badge.tsx, which shadcn generates, and it's the
    standard shadcn pattern. I've left the generated files unedited.
  - README protected: I scaffolded in a temporary folder, so the template's
    README couldn't overwrite ours.

  - npm run lint: 0 errors. The one warning is still in shadcn's generated
    badge.tsx.
  - npm run build: the type check and production build pass.

  Your focus areas, and the test that guards each:
  - Rounding: every figure is checked to the cent against the acceptance
    criteria. The figures came from the separate reference calculator, not from
    the code being tested.
  - Zero interest: for every loan length and several amounts near the minimum,
    total interest is exactly 0.00. A 1,000 loan over 3 months comes out as
    333.33, 333.33, 333.34.
  - Final payment: it is always positive near the 1,000 minimum, across every
    rate and length. It equals the EMI on a one-month loan, and matches the
    expected values at the largest inputs.
  - Fees: changing the fee leaves the EMI and total repaid unchanged. Also
    covered: switching between % and fixed, an empty fee counting as 0, a fee
    one cent under the loan, and a fee that becomes invalid when the amount
    drops.
  - Ties: two-way and three-way ties, and an EMI tie caused by different fees on
    the same loan. In that last case one offer gets "Lowest total cost" and
    both get "Lowest monthly payment (tied)".

  How I checked that the tests can fail

  Everything passed on the first run, which on its own doesn't show the tests
  can catch anything. So I planted six realistic bugs, one at a time, and every
  one caused failures:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┐
  │                 Planted bug                  │ Tests that failed │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Going back to total repaid = EMI × months    │ 30                │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Percentage fee 10× too large                 │ 14                │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ No special case for 0% interest              │ 5                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Ties not marked "(tied)"                     │ 3                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ 3 decimal places allowed                     │ 3                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ A fixed fee equal to the loan amount allowed │ 1                 │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────┘

  Issues and decisions to review

  1. The README's final-payment claim says that for typical offers the final
     payment is within 1.00 of the EMI. My earlier evidence was only a random
     sample, so I rechecked it on a dense grid of about 1 million offers; the
     worst case was 0.95. It's a measured result rather than a proof, so there's
     now a test that guards it.
  2. Behaviour not covered by the acceptance criteria:
     - 12. and .5 are accepted, so the figures don't vanish while you're still
       typing 12.5. 12.0 counts as a whole number of months.
     - A fixed fee is only checked against the amount once the amount itself is
       valid. For example, with an amount of 999.99 and a fixed fee of 5,000,
       you see only the amount message. The fee message appears once the amount
       is fixed, if the fee is still too big.
  3. One known limit, marked in the code: the EMI formula is the only step that
     uses floating-point maths. A result within about a millionth of a half cent
     could round the wrong way. No tested case comes near that, and I left a
     ponytail: comment explaining what to switch to if it ever matters.
  4. One test is true by design: S-2.2 (the totals add up) holds because of how
     the totals are calculated. It's still there to catch a future refactor that
     breaks it.
  5. Separation confirmed: domain/ has no React or browser code. The logic is
     239 lines across 5 files, and the test examples match the docs.

         +t }] : []

         +Cost === null)

         +ult.emi))

         +.emi === lowestEmi)                                            
      65 +

         +me="mt-8 mb-6 min-h-16 sm:mt-10 sm:mb-8">

         +">{comparison.message}</p>}

         +m sm:text-2xl">                                                
      72 +            <mark className="marker">{names(cheapest)}</mark> {
         +cheapest.length > 1 ? 'tie for' : 'has'} the lowest total      
      73 +            cost: <span className="tabular-nums">{formatMoney(c
         +heapest[0].result.totalCost)}</span>.                          
      74 +          </p>

         +ground">

         +1 ? 'tie for' : 'has'} the lowest monthly payment:{' '}

         +mi)}</span>.

       28 +                                                              
       29 +async function enter(n: number, { amount, rate, length, years,
          + fee, fixedFee }: Entry) {                                    
       30 +  if (amount) await user.type(box(n, 'Amount borrowed'), amoun
          +t)                                                            
       31 +  if (rate) await user.type(box(n, 'Annual interest rate (%)')
          +, rate)                                                       
       32 +  if (length) await user.type(box(n, 'Loan length'), length)  
       33 +  if (years) await user.selectOptions(box(n, 'Loan length unit
          +'), 'years')                                                  
       34 +  if (fixedFee) await user.selectOptions(box(n, 'Upfront fee t
          +ype'), 'fixed')                                               
       35 +  if (fee) await user.type(box(n, 'Upfront fee'), fee)        
       36 +}                                                             
       37 +                                                              
       38 +async function replace(n: number, label: string, text: string)
          + {                                                            
       39 +  await user.clear(box(n, label))                             
       40 +  if (text) await user.type(box(n, label), text)              
       41 +}

⏺ Everything passes, so I'll commit the interface stage.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The interface is built and connected to the existing logic, and it's
  committed. The dev server is running at http://localhost:5180. Port 5173 was
  already taken by another Node process on your machine (PID 28346). It isn't
  ours, so I left it alone.

  What it looks like

  - Header: the headline asks "Which loan offer really costs less?". Beside it
    on desktop, or under it on a phone, a summary answers live: "Offer 1 has the
    lowest total cost: 53,582.58. Offer 3 has the lowest monthly payment:
    5,561.11." It also handles ties, and it shows the one-offer and
    different-amounts messages.
  - Offer cards: three cards, side by side on desktop and stacked on a phone.
    What you type sits on white; what it costs sits in a shaded panel
    underneath.
  - Desktop alignment: results start at the same height in every column, so you
    can read straight across.
  - Inside each card:
    - The monthly payment is the largest number, then the total cost of
      borrowing.
    - Below them, a figures list reads like a bank statement: amount borrowed,
      minus the fee, gives the amount received, set off with a subtotal line.
    - Then total interest and total repaid.
  - One accent colour: a highlighter yellow marks only the lowest total cost. It
    sweeps in once and is switched off for users who've asked their device to
    reduce motion.
  - Font: Instrument Sans, self-hosted, with a narrower width for the large
    figures. All numbers use equal-width digits, which I measured in the
    browser.
  - Phone details:
    - 16px inputs, so iOS doesn't zoom in when you tap a box.
    - Number keypads, and the phone's own pickers for months/years and %/fixed.
    - Every field is at least 44px tall, so it's easy to tap.

  The design review in a headless browser led to two fixes:
  - Gap under the intro: a large empty space on desktop is gone, because the
    summary now sits beside the headline.
  - Uneven rows: the figures under the totals sat at different heights across
    offers. They now line up.

  Checks

  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Check       │                        Result                        │

  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

  │ Chromium         │ 375px                                                │
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The 15 interface tests cover these flows:
  - Calculating offers: example A typed in live, switching to years, the final
    payment line, and raising the fee without changing the EMI.
  - When messages appear: they wait until you leave the box, clear as you
    correct it, and leave unvisited boxes alone.
  - Fee check: lowering the amount below a fixed fee, then fixing the fee.
  - Comparison: labels, ties, re-ranking after a change, and different amounts.
  - Privacy: no network calls while typing.

  To check the interface tests can fail, I planted two bugs: messages showing
  immediately, and no highlighter. Each made tests fail.

  What still needs testing in a real browser

  Everything so far ran in headless Chromium only. Before any story gets marked
  Implemented, these need a hand check:

  1. Cross-browser: Firefox and Safari. The aligned columns, the narrower
     figures and the highlighter animation have only been seen in Chromium.
  2. A real phone (iPhone Safari and Android Chrome) for S-1.11:
     - no zoom when you tap a box
     - the number keypad appears
     - the native pickers work
     - the keyboard doesn't hide the box you're typing in
  3. S-1.12 keyboard: Tab goes through Offer 1, then 2, then 3, and the focus
     ring is visible at each step.
  4. S-1.10: with the browser language set to German, amounts still show as
     8,884.88.
  5. S-8.1 to S-8.4:

⏺ Archivo is installed: weight 100–900, width 62–125%. Domain tests pass (99,
  including the new $ test).

  New design plan
  - Concept: you type the offer, the page gives you the answer. Each offer is a
    sentence you fill in, like reading your offer letter back: "Borrow 
    [$250,000] at [12.5%] a year, repaid over [36] [months], with an upfront fee
    of [1] [% of amount]." The results card is the focus of the page:
    - an ultramarine top panel holding the answer: one large monthly payment for
      a single offer, or the verdict when comparing
    - a white body showing where every repaid dollar goes in a bar split into
      upfront fee, interest and cash received
    - when comparing, all offers' bars share one scale, so the cost difference
      is visible at a glance
  - Palette:
    - ultramarine #1F33CF for brand and the answer panel
    - deep ink #0B1236 for text
    - mint #6DF0BE, used only to mark the cheapest offer
    - bar colours as validated: fee #E8A13A, interest #D6336C, received #5C6CF0
  - Type: Archivo throughout. The headline and the large figures are set
    slightly wide and heavy, so the numbers read as the main content.
  - Layout:
    - desktop: offer sentences in a 30rem left column with "Add another offer"
      under them, and the results card filling the right
    - mobile: offers, then results, then the add button
    - number of offers: the page starts with one offer; up to three can be added
      and removed again
  - Interactions:
    - a new offer starts with the previous offer's amount filled in, and the
      cursor goes straight to its rate
    - after removing an offer, the cursor moves to the add button
    - bars resize smoothly when figures change
    - the mint marker sweeps in on the cheapest offer
    - all motion is switched off for users who've asked their device to reduce
      motion
  - Avoiding a templated look: no all-caps labels, no dot-separated details, no
    gradients, no arrows on buttons, no monospace. shadcn drops out completely,
    because inputs inside sentences and native dropdowns don't fit its
    components.

  Writing the styles and components now:

       1  # Loan & EMI Calculator

       3 -A browser-based calculator for people comparing personal loan o
         -ffers. Enter up to three offers and see, for each one, the mont
         -hly payment (EMI), the cash you actually receive after the upfr
         -ont fee, and the total cost of borrowing, with the lowest total
         - cost and the lowest monthly payment clearly labelled.         
       3 +A browser-based calculator for people choosing a personal loan.
         + Start with one offer and see the monthly payment (EMI), the ca
         +sh you actually receive after the upfront fee, the total cost o
         +f borrowing, and where every repaid dollar goes. Add up to two 
         +more offers to compare them: the lowest total cost and the lowe
         +st monthly payment are clearly labelled, and every offer is dra
         +wn to the same scale.

      48  ## Terms used in the app

     ...
       67  - Fixed interest rate for the whole loan, charged on the reduc
           ing balance, with one payment a month.
       68  - EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n / ((1 + r)^n − 1), where P is the am
           ount borrowed, r is the annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100, and n is the n
           umber of months. At 0% interest, EMI = P ÷ n.
       69  - **The upfront fee does not change the EMI.** The EMI is alwa
           ys calculated on the full amount borrowed, not the amount rece
           ived.
       70 -- **Rounding follows lender practice.** The EMI is rounded to 
          -the nearest 0.01 (halves round up). Each month's interest is c
          -harged on the remaining balance and rounded to the nearest 0.0
          -1. The final payment is whatever clears the balance exactly, s
          -o it can differ slightly from the EMI.                        
       71 -- Every amount shown is exact to 0.01, and the figures always 
          -add up: total repaid = EMI × (months − 1) + final payment, and
          - total interest = total repaid − amount borrowed. At 0% intere
          -st, total interest is exactly 0.00.                           
       72 -- We do not use total repaid = EMI × months. With a rounded EM
          -I, that formula overstates or understates the real total and b
          -reaks at the edges. For example, borrowing 100 at 0% over 3 mo
          -nths gives an EMI of 33.33, and 33.33 × 3 = 99.99, which shows
          - −0.01 interest on an interest-free loan.                     
       73 -- A percentage fee is calculated on the amount borrowed and ro
          -unded to the nearest 0.01.                                    
       74 -- Lenders round in different ways (some to whole units), so th
          -eir figures may differ from ours by a small amount. For typica
          -l offers (10–36% interest, 12–60 months) the final payment is 
          -within 1.00 of the EMI.                                       
       70 +- **Rounding follows lender practice.** The EMI is rounded to 
          +the nearest cent (halves round up). Each month's interest is c
          +harged on the remaining balance and rounded to the nearest cen
          +t. The final payment is whatever clears the balance exactly, s
          +o it can differ slightly from the EMI.                        
       71 +- Every amount shown is exact to the cent, and the figures alw
          +ays add up: total repaid = EMI × (months − 1) + final payment,
          + and total interest = total repaid − amount borrowed. At 0% in
          +terest, total interest is exactly $0.00.                      
       72 +- We do not use total repaid = EMI × months. With a rounded EM
          +I, that formula overstates or understates the real total and b
          +reaks at the edges. For example, borrowing $100 at 0% over 3 m
          +onths gives an EMI of $33.33, and $33.33 × 3 = $99.99, which s
          +hows −$0.01 interest on an interest-free loan.                
       73 +- A percentage fee is calculated on the amount borrowed and ro
          +unded to the nearest cent.                                    
       74 +- Lenders round in different ways (some to whole dollars), so 
          +their figures may differ from ours by a small amount. For typi
          +cal offers (10–36% interest, 12–60 months) the final payment i
          +s within $1.00 of the EMI.

       76  ### Inputs
       77  
       78  | Input | Allowed values |
       79  |---|---|
       80 -| Amount borrowed | 1,000 to 100,000,000, up to 2 decimal plac
          -es |                                                          
       80 +| Amount borrowed | $1,000 to $100,000,000, up to 2 decimal pl
          +aces |                                                        
       81  | Annual interest rate | 0% to 50%, up to 2 decimal places |
       82  | Loan length | A whole number: 1 to 120 months, or 1 to 10 ye
           ars |
       83  | Upfront fee (percentage) | 0% to 10% of the amount borrowed,
            up to 2 decimal places |
       84 -| Upfront fee (fixed amount) | 0 or more and less than the amo
          -unt borrowed, up to 2 decimal places |                        
       84 +| Upfront fee (fixed amount) | $0 or more and less than the am
          +ount borrowed, up to 2 decimal places |                       
       85  
       86 -- **Product decision:** the minimum amount borrowed is 1,000. 
          -Personal loans smaller than that are rare in most currencies, 
          -and the app is for people comparing real offers.              
       87 -- Plain numbers only: an optional minus sign, digits, one opti
          -onal decimal point, and optional commas as thousands separator
          -s. Commas and spaces at either end are ignored, so both `500,0
          -00` and `5,00,000` work. Anything else, such as letters, `%` o
          -r `1e5`, is rejected with a message. A negative number gets th
          -e box's range message.                                        
       88 -- Amount borrowed, interest rate and loan length are required.
          - The upfront fee is optional: an empty fee counts as 0.       
       86 +- **Product decision:** the minimum amount borrowed is $1,000.
          + Personal loans smaller than that are rare, and the app is for
          + people comparing real offers.                                
       87 +- Plain numbers only: an optional minus sign, digits, one opti
          +onal decimal point, and optional commas as thousands separator
          +s. Commas and spaces at either end are ignored, so both `500,0
          +00` and `5,00,000` work. The money boxes show a `$` beside the
          +m, and a `$` typed in front of the number there is ignored too
          +. Anything else, such as letters, `%` or `1e5`, is rejected wi
          +th a message. A negative number gets the box's range message. 
       88 +- Amount borrowed, interest rate and loan length are required.
          + The upfront fee is optional: an empty fee counts as $0.      
       89  - Loan length starts in months and the upfront fee starts as a
            percentage. Switching to years, or to a fixed amount, keeps t
           he number typed and changes only what it means.
       90  - Messages appear when the user leaves a box. At that moment, 
           every box in the offer that the user has visited and that is i
           nvalid shows its message, so lowering the amount borrowed belo
           w a fixed fee reveals the fee message (S-3.7). Changing months
           /years or %/fixed counts as leaving that box. A message that i
           s showing updates as the user types and disappears as soon as 
           the value is valid. An offer's figures are hidden the moment a
           ny of its boxes is missing or invalid, even before a message a
           ppears.
       91 -- Each offer is validated on its own. An offer with every fiel
          -d empty counts as not entered: it shows no messages and no res
          -ults. An offer with any missing or invalid field shows its mes
          -sages and no results, and is left out of the comparison; the o
          -ther offers still work.                                       
       91 +- Each offer is validated on its own. An offer with every box 
          +empty counts as not entered: it shows no messages and no resul
          +ts. An offer with any missing or invalid box shows its message
          +s and no results, and is left out of the comparison; the other
          + offers still work.

       94  
       95 -- Currency-neutral: no currency symbol. Amounts are always sho

          -, two decimal places) whatever the browser's language.        
       95 +- Every amount is shown in dollars, as `$1,234,567.89`, whatev
          +er the browser's language. The `$` is a display choice only: n
          +othing is converted, and the maths is the same in any currency
          +.

       97 +### Offers and comparison                                     
       98  
       99 -- Three offers (Offer 1 to Offer 3) are always on the page; an
          -y of them can be left empty. Only valid offers are compared, a
          -nd labels and differences update as soon as any offer changes.
      100 -- **No valid offers:** no results and no labels.              
      101 -- **One valid offer:** its results are shown with the note "Ad
          -d another offer to compare." There are no labels.             
       99 +- The page starts with one offer. **Add another offer** adds o
          +ne, up to three. A new offer starts with the previous offer's 
          +amount borrowed when that amount is valid, because offers bein
          +g compared are usually for the same loan, and the cursor goes 
          +to its interest rate.                                         
      100 +- Any offer can be removed while there are at least two. Offer
          +s are numbered by position, so removing Offer 2 makes Offer 3 
          +the new Offer 2. After a removal the cursor goes to **Add anot
          +her offer**.                                                  
      101 +- Only valid offers are compared, and labels and differences u
          +pdate as soon as any offer changes.                           
      102 +- **No valid offers:** no figures and no labels.              
      103 +- **One valid offer:** its results are shown in full with the 
          +note "Add another offer to compare." There are no labels.     
      104  - **Different amounts borrowed:** offers for different amounts
            cannot be ranked fairly. If the valid offers are not all for 
           the same amount borrowed, each offer's results are still shown
           , but there are no labels or differences, and a message asks f
           or the same amount borrowed in every offer. Loan lengths and f
           ees may differ; showing that trade-off is the point.
      103 -- **Labels:** "Lowest total cost" goes to the offer with the l
          -owest total cost of borrowing. "Lowest monthly payment" goes t
          -o the offer with the lowest EMI (not the final payment). One o
          -ffer can carry both.                                          
      104 -- **Ranking uses the amounts shown**, rounded to 0.01, so a la
          -bel never contradicts the figures on screen.                  
      105 -- **Ties:** every offer that shares the lowest value gets the 
          -label with "(tied)" added, for example "Lowest total cost (tie
          -d)". If all valid offers tie, they all get it.                
      106 -- **Differences:** each offer without the "Lowest total cost" 
          -label shows how much more its total cost of borrowing is than 
          -the lowest, for example "Costs 1,250.00 more in total". Offers
          - tied for lowest show no difference.                          
      105 +- **Labels:** "Lowest total cost" goes to the offer with the l
          +owest total cost of borrowing. "Lowest monthly payment" goes t
          +o the offer with the lowest EMI (not the final payment). One o
          +ffer can carry both. A summary states the outcome, for example
          + "Offer 1 has the lowest total cost: $53,582.58."             
      106 +- **Ranking uses the amounts shown**, rounded to the cent, so 
          +a label never contradicts the figures on screen.              
      107 +- **Ties:** every offer that shares the lowest value gets the 
          +label with "(tied)" added, for example "Lowest total cost (tie
          +d)", and the summary says the offers tie. If all valid offers 
          +tie, they all get it.                                         
      108 +- **Differences:** each offer without the "Lowest total cost" 
          +label shows how much more its total cost of borrowing is than 
          +the lowest, for example "Costs $1,250.00 more in total". Offer
          +s tied for lowest show no difference.                         
      109 +- **Repayment bar:** each offer's total repaid is drawn as a b
          +ar split into the upfront fee, the total interest and the amou
          +nt received, with the cost parts first. When comparing, every 
          +bar uses the same scale, so the cost parts can be compared by 
          +length.

      123 -_To be written once the app is built._                        
      136 +```bash                                                       
      137 +npm test                                                      
      138 +```                                                           
      139  
      140 +Runs every test once with Vitest, with no browser needed:     
      141 +                                                              
      142 +- `src/domain/*.test.ts`: the calculation, validation and comp
          +arison rules. Each test is named after the acceptance criterio
          +n it checks, and the expected figures were worked out separate
          +ly before the code was written.                               
      143 +- `src/App.test.tsx`: the main flows in the interface (typing 
          +an offer, when messages appear, adding and removing offers, th
          +e comparison), using Testing Library.                         
      144 +                                                              
      145 +`npm run lint` runs oxlint. `npm run build` also type-checks. 
      146 +                                                              
      147 +Automated tests do not replace the hand checks in a real brows
          +er listed in the user stories (phone, keyboard, browser langua
          +ge, network).                                                 
      148 +                                                              
      149 +## How the code is organised                                  
      150 +                                                              
      151 +- `src/domain/`: plain TypeScript with no React. Validation tu
          +rns typed text into whole cents and basis points, the loan cal
          +culation uses whole cents throughout, and the comparison ranks
          + the results. All user-facing messages are in `messages.ts`.  
      152 +- `src/components/OfferEditor.tsx`: one offer, written as a se
          +ntence with the boxes filled in, plus the rule for when messag
          +es appear.                                                    
      153 +- `src/components/Results.tsx`: the results panel, the repayme
          +nt bar and the comparison.                                    
      154 +- `src/App.tsx`: the page. Only the typed text is state; valid
          +ation, results and the comparison are worked out from it on ev
          +ery change.

      23  
      24  ## How to read the criteria
      25  
      25 -- The page shows three offers: **Offer 1**, **Offer 2** and **O
         -ffer 3**. Each has four boxes:                                 
      26 -  - **Amount borrowed**                                        
      27 -  - **Annual interest rate (%)**                               
      28 -  - **Loan length**, with a choice of months or years (starts o
         -n months)                                                      
      29 -  - **Upfront fee**, with a choice of % of amount or fixed amou
         -nt (starts on %)                                               
      26 +- The page opens with one offer, **Offer 1**. **Add another off
         +er** adds Offer 2 and then Offer 3; **Remove** takes an offer a
         +way again.                                                     
      27 +- Each offer is a sentence with four boxes and two choices: "Bo
         +rrow **$[Amount borrowed]** at **[Annual interest rate (%)]%** 
         +a year, repaid over **[Loan length]** **[months/years]**, with 
         +an upfront fee of **[Upfront fee]** **[% of amount / fixed amou
         +nt]**." Loan length starts on months and the fee starts on % of
         + amount.                                                       
      28  - "Enter" means typing into the boxes. There is no Calculate bu
          tton; figures appear and update as you type.
      29  - "Leave the box" means moving focus out of it, for example by 
          pressing Tab.
      32 -- Amounts are shown with comma thousands separators and two dec
         -imal places, for example `8,884.88`.                           
      30 +- Figures appear in the **Results** panel, beside the offers on
         + desktop and below them on a phone. With one valid offer, that 
         +offer's results fill the panel. With more, each valid offer has
         + its own section, under a summary of the outcome. "Offer 1 show
         +s …" in a results criterion means Offer 1's results.           
      31 +- Every amount is shown in dollars with comma thousands separat
         +ors and two decimal places, for example `$8,884.88`. The money 
         +boxes show a `$` beside them; the `$` does not need to be typed
         +.                                                              
      32  
      33  ### Example offers
      34  
      36 -Criteria refer to these by name. "Enter example A in Offer 1" m
         -eans typing these values into Offer 1's boxes.                 
      35 +Criteria refer to these by name. "Enter example A in Offer 1" m
         +eans filling Offer 1's boxes with these values.                
      36  
      37  | Example | Amount borrowed | Annual interest rate | Loan lengt
          h | Upfront fee |
      38  |---|---|---|---|---|
      40 -| A | 100,000 | 12 | 12 months | 2 (%) |                       
      41 -| B1 | 250,000 | 12.5 | 36 months | 1 (%) |                    
      42 -| B2 | 250,000 | 12 | 36 months | 3 (%) |                      
      43 -| B3 | 250,000 | 12 | 60 months | empty |                      
      39 +| A | $100,000 | 12% | 12 months | 2 (% of amount) |           
      40 +| B1 | $250,000 | 12.5% | 36 months | 1 (% of amount) |        
      41 +| B2 | $250,000 | 12% | 36 months | 3 (% of amount) |          
      42 +| B3 | $250,000 | 12% | 60 months | empty |

           of borrowing |

      49 -| A | 8,884.88 | 8,884.85 | 106,618.53 | 6,618.53 | 2,000.00 | 
         -98,000.00 | 8,618.53 |                                         
      50 -| B1 | 8,363.41 | 8,363.23 | 301,082.58 | 51,082.58 | 2,500.00 
         -| 247,500.00 | 53,582.58 |

         -| 242,500.00 | 56,428.73 |                                     
      52 -| B3 | 5,561.11 | 5,561.29 | 333,666.78 | 83,666.78 | 0.00 | 25
         -0,000.00 | 83,666.78 |                                         
      48 +| A | $8,884.88 | $8,884.85 | $106,618.53 | $6,618.53 | $2,000.
         +00 | $98,000.00 | $8,618.53 |                                  
      49 +| B1 | $8,363.41 | $8,363.23 | $301,082.58 | $51,082.58 | $2,50
         +0.00 | $247,500.00 | $53,582.58 |                              
      50 +| B2 | $8,303.58 | $8,303.43 | $298,928.73 | $48,928.73 | $7,50
         +0.00 | $242,500.00 | $56,428.73 |                              
      51 +| B3 | $5,561.11 | $5,561.29 | $333,666.78 | $83,666.78 | $0.00
         + | $250,000.00 | $83,666.78 |

           the lowest monthly payment but costs the most.

       61  | Loan length | Left empty while another box in the offer has 
           a value | Enter the loan length. |
       62  | Amount borrowed, interest rate or upfront fee | Not a number
            | Enter a number using only digits, a decimal point and comma
           s. |
       63  | Amount borrowed, interest rate or upfront fee | More than 2 
           decimal places | Use no more than 2 decimal places. |
       65 -| Amount borrowed | Outside 1,000 to 100,000,000 | Enter an am
          -ount between 1,000 and 100,000,000. |                         
       64 +| Amount borrowed | Outside $1,000 to $100,000,000 | Enter an 
          +amount between $1,000 and $100,000,000. |                     
       65  | Annual interest rate | Outside 0 to 50 | Enter a rate betwee
           n 0% and 50%. |
       66  | Loan length in months | Not a whole number from 1 to 120 | E
           nter a whole number of months between 1 and 120. |
       67  | Loan length in years | Not a whole number from 1 to 10 | Ent
           er a whole number of years between 1 and 10. |
       68  | Upfront fee as % | Outside 0 to 10 | Enter a fee between 0% 
           and 10%. |
       70 -| Upfront fee as fixed amount | Negative, or not less than the
          - amount borrowed | Enter a fee of 0 or more, less than the amo
          -unt borrowed. |                                               
       71 -| An offer's results | The offer is empty, incomplete or has a
          -n invalid box | Enter the amount borrowed, interest rate and l
          -oan length to see results. |                                  
       72 -| Comparison | Exactly one valid offer | Add another offer to 
          -compare. |                                                    
       73 -| Comparison | Valid offers have different amounts borrowed | 
          -These offers are for different amounts, so they can't be ranke
          -d. Use the same amount borrowed in every offer to compare them
          -. |                                                           
       69 +| Upfront fee as fixed amount | Negative, or not less than the
          + amount borrowed | Enter a fee of $0 or more, less than the am
          +ount borrowed. |                                              
       70 +| Under an offer | The offer is empty, incomplete or has an in
          +valid box | Enter the amount borrowed, interest rate and loan 
          +length to see results. |                                      
       71 +| Results panel | Exactly one valid offer | Add another offer 
          +to compare. |                                                 
       72 +| Results panel | Valid offers have different amounts borrowed
          + | These offers are for different amounts, so they can't be ra
          +nked. Use the same amount borrowed in every offer to compare t
          +hem. |                                                        
       73  | Page | Always | Estimates only. Your lender's figures may di
           ffer slightly because of rounding. |
