  error messages and red fields show, the tablet focus ring is visible, and the
  console is clean. I'm now committing the redesign and the dollar display
  together so each commit keeps the tests green.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The redesign is built, committed and running at http://localhost:5180. The
  screenshots above are the new design at desktop, tablet and phone widths. The
  calculation and validation logic is unchanged apart from two small changes
  (dollar formatting, and accepting a typed $), and all 118 tests pass.

  How the experience works now

  - One offer to start. The page opens with one offer. Add another offer adds up
    to three, and any offer can be removed while at least two remain.
    - A new offer starts with the previous offer's amount and puts the cursor
      straight in its rate, since compared offers are usually for the same loan.
    - Removing an offer renumbers the rest and moves the cursor to the add
      button.
  - Each offer is a sentence you fill in: "Borrow $250,000 at 12.5% a year,
    repaid over 36 months, with an upfront fee of 1 % of amount." It reads like
    your offer letter rather than a form, and on phones it only breaks lines at
    natural points.
  - The results are the main focus. On desktop they sit in a large panel beside
    the offers and start at the top of the page; on phones they follow the
    offers.
    - One offer: a large monthly payment and total cost, then "Where your 
      repayments go", a bar splitting the total repaid into upfront fee,
      interest and cash received.
    - Two or three offers: a summary at the top ("Offer 1 has the lowest total
      cost: $53,582.58"), then each offer with its labels, its "Costs $X more"
      line and its bar.
    - Shared scale: all bars use one scale, so the cost of a longer loan is
      visible at a glance.
  - Visual identity:
    - an ultramarine answer panel, with mint used only to mark the cheapest
      offer
    - Archivo, set heavy and slightly wide for the headline and figures
    - three bar colours checked with the data-visualisation palette validator,
      with figures beside the bar acting as its legend
    - one sweep animation on the cheapest offer, and smooth bar changes, all
      switched off when the system asks for reduced motion
  - Dollars everywhere: $1,234.56 in every result, message, comparison and money
    box. It's display only, with no conversion.

  Decisions to check

  1. A typed $ is now accepted in the two money boxes, so $250,000 works. That's
     the only change to the validation rules, and I made it because the boxes
     show a $ and people will type one.
  2. shadcn/ui is gone completely. Boxes inside a sentence and the phone's own
     dropdowns don't fit its components, so nothing was left where it helped.
  3. The README is now complete: prerequisites, install and run commands, tests,
     and how the code is organised. "Charts" is out of the Not planned list,
     since the app now has one.
  4. The user stories now match the build:
     - dollars throughout
     - new S-12 (add and remove offers)
     - new S-3.9 (the bar) and S-4.9 (the shared scale)
     - the summary sentences added to S-4, S-5 and S-7
     - S-1.12, S-4.5, S-4.7, S-6.6 and S-8.3 updated for starting with one offer

     All stories are still Not implemented until they've been checked by hand.

  Checks

  - Tests: 118 pass (99 logic, 19 interface).
  - Can the new tests fail? I removed the amount prefill and the cursor move
    after removal, one at a time; tests caught each.
  - Lint, build, audit: lint clean, build passes, 0 vulnerabilities.
  - Headless Chromium:
    - no console errors
    - no sideways scrolling at 375px, even at the largest values
      ($413,756,375.72)
    - the keyboard focus ring is visible

  Still to test in a real browser

  - Firefox and Safari: large figures shrink to fit their column using a newer
    CSS feature (container units), and the headline uses a wider font width.
    Both were checked only in Chromium.
  - A real phone: the boxes inside sentences with the on-screen keyboard open,
    the number keypads, and the phone's own pickers for months/years and fee
    type.
  - VoiceOver: each box is announced by its label (for example "Amount
    borrowed"), and the summary is read out when the result changes.
  - Earlier checks, still pending: German browser language, the network search
    for 123456.78, working offline, reloading, and reduced motion.

  Once you've checked these, I'll mark the stories that pass as Implemented.

⏺ I'll polish rather than rethink. Here's the plan, then I'll check the dark bar
  colours before writing anything:

  - Theme: an inline script sets the theme before the first paint, so there's no
    flash. It uses your saved choice if there is one, otherwise the system
    setting, and it keeps following the system until you pick. The toggle in the
    header saves your choice.
  - Dark palette: deep navy, built as a second set of colour values so the
    components themselves don't change. The bar colours get re-checked against
    the dark background.
  - Highlights: both labels become the same subtle pill in two colours. Green
    means lowest total cost, blue means lowest monthly payment. The winning
    figure gets a thin underline in its label's colour. The heavy mint block
    goes.
  - Footer: clean, with the wordmark and the disclaimer. The privacy lines come
    out of the header and footer.
  - Playwright:
    - tests run against the production build in Chromium, in both a desktop
      project and a Pixel 7 mobile project
    - specs cover calculations, offers, comparison, validation, theme and mobile
      layout
    - several checks that were hand-only can now be automated in Chromium: the
      network check, offline, reload, the German browser language, and keyboard
      order

      2  
      3  A browser-based calculator for people choosing a personal loan. 
         Start with one offer and see the monthly payment (EMI), the cash
          you actually receive after the upfront fee, the total cost of b
         orrowing, and where every repaid dollar goes. Add up to two more
          offers to compare them: the lowest total cost and the lowest mo
         nthly payment are clearly labelled, and every offer is drawn to 
         the same scale.

⏺ The theme switch, the subtler highlights and the new footer are done.
  Playwright end-to-end tests now run in Chromium: all 143 pass, the 118
  existing tests still pass, and lint, build and audit are clean. Your dev
  server at http://localhost:5180 has the latest version.

  What changed

  - Light/dark switch: a sun/moon button at the top right.
    - The first visit follows the system setting, and keeps following it until
      you choose.
    - Your choice is saved in the browser and wins over the system from then on.

      colours. A test checks this with the app's script blocked.
  - Deep navy dark theme. I re-checked the bar colours against the navy with the
    data-visualisation validator; only the amber had to get deeper. Every text
    colour pair passes the WCAG AA contrast minimum in both themes.
  - Badges and highlights: both labels are now the same small pill with a dot,
    green for lowest total cost and blue for lowest monthly payment. The winning
    figure gets a thin underline in the same colour instead of a highlighter
    block, and the summary names the winner in mint.
  - Footer: full width, with the wordmark and the disclaimer. The privacy lines
    are gone from the header and footer.

  Playwright tests

  They run against the production build at two sizes: desktop (1440 × 900) and a
  Pixel 7 phone. Each test is named after the story criterion it covers. They
  cover:
  - calculations
  - adding and removing offers
  - comparison
  - all 27 error messages
  - the theme switch
  - phone layout at 375px in both themes
  - privacy

  Several checks I'd listed as hand-only are now automated in Chromium:
  - the German browser language (S-1.10)
  - no sideways scrolling (S-1.11)
  - Tab order (S-1.12)
  - no typed values in any request, working offline, and reload clearing
    everything (S-8.1–8.3)

  Bugs the tests found, now fixed

  1. Boxes were hard to tap on a phone. The input inside each 44px box was only
     20px tall, so tapping the edge or the $ did nothing. The whole box now
     responds.
  2. Clicks on "Add another offer" could be lost. If you'd just typed something
     invalid, pressing the button showed the error message first, which pushed
     the button down before the click landed. Remove had the same problem. Both
     buttons now keep focus where it was when pressed, so nothing moves
     mid-click.
  3. Smaller fixes from reviewing the screenshots:
     - Remove was below the 44px tap minimum.
     - Error and focus rings crowded the words next to them.
     - There was a gap before % in the rate box.

  Two of the first failures were mistakes in my tests, not the app. One measured
  the bars before they'd finished animating; the other pressed Tab once too
  often.

  Screenshots reviewed

  16 screenshots, covering the empty page, one offer, three offers and errors,
  in both themes at both sizes. The ones above are from that set. They're
  regenerated on every run at
  /Users/sallar/Downloads/KnowledgeCity/test-results/screens/, which git
  ignores.

  Decisions to check

  - No user story for the theme. Under the assignment's rule that every story
    must serve a job, a display preference doesn't qualify. The README says so,
    and the end-to-end tests cover it.
  - The theme choice is stored. It's the only thing the app saves in the
    browser, and the README's privacy note now says so. Nothing you type is
    stored.

  - Firefox and Safari
  - a real phone's on-screen keyboard
  - VoiceOver
  - marking stories Implemented once they pass

⏺ Two things to plan around:

  Order: you asked for missing behaviour first and design after, so:
  1. An audit of every MVP acceptance criterion: one Playwright test per
     criterion, run in a real browser, with a pass/fail table.
  2. Fixes for any missing behaviour, plus the misleading wording.
  3. Story statuses and docs based on that evidence.
  4. Then the dropdowns.

  The repayment breakdown is wrong as worded. The UI says "Every repayment is
  split into the upfront fee, the interest and the cash you actually received".
  That's false: the upfront fee is taken out of the loan before it's paid out,
  so it isn't part of any monthly repayment. The bar shows that the total repaid
  equals cash received + upfront fee + interest. The fee is in that total
  because you repay money you never received. I'll correct this in the app, the
  README and the stories.

  The QA agent is still running. Its Playwright runs build into the shared dist/
  folder and serve it on port 4321. To avoid clobbering each other, my audit
  runs use a separate build folder and port. I'll also hold off changing app

  Before I change any app code or docs, I'm waiting for the QA agent to finish.

  - Explanation under it: "You repay $106,618.53 in total: the $98,000.00 you

    interest)."

› Message from general-purpose
  # QA audit: Loan & EMI Calculator (KnowledgeCity take-home)

  I couldn't save this report to `scratchpad/qa/report-1.md`: the harness
  refused the write ("Subagents should return findings as text"). The full
  report follows here.

  Audited: `main` at `8383bc3` (src/ unchanged during the audit). Chromium
  (desktop 1440×900 and Pixel 7) plus WebKit (desktop Safari and iPhone 13).
  Firefox could not be launched in my sandbox, so it is **unverified**.

  Scratch folder (all paths below that start with `scratch/` mean this folder):
  `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-sallar-Downloads-KnowledgeCity/ea904531-f839-4
  cf2-bff2-d1f467cc45b0/scratchpad/qa/`

  ---

  ## 1. Summary verdict

  **Not ready to submit as it stands, but close.** The app itself is solid:
  - I found no crash, console error or dialog in about 1,100 browser test runs,
  including fuzzing.
  - The calculations match an independent decimal reference in 41,949 of 41,950
  sampled cases.
  - Every MVP acceptance criterion I automated passes in Chromium and WebKit.

  What would cost points in review is mostly the paperwork, not the app:
  1. **Every user story is marked "Not implemented"** even though the app does
  what they describe (QA-1). The brief scores accuracy and says to mark status
  "based on what the app does today".
  2. **The README's Node prerequisite is wrong**: `npm test` fails outright on
  Node 22.12 to 22.14, which the README says are supported (QA-2).
  3. **The README links `transcripts/`, but that folder does not exist** (QA-3).
  Transcripts are a required deliverable.

  Real but narrow app defects:
  - A 1-cent EMI rounding error on some 1-month loans (QA-4).
  - Comma-decimal input accepted silently as a 10× or 100× value (QA-5, a
  documented design risk).
  - Figures overprint each other in the comparison view at 1024px for very large
  loans (QA-6).
  - No visible focus on a box in its error state (QA-7).
  - Sideways scrolling at 320px with realistic fixed fees (QA-8).
  - Several low-severity accessibility and layout items.

  Once QA-1 to QA-3 are fixed, I would expect this to pass review. QA-4 and QA-5
  are the only findings that touch the brief's "never a wrong result" rule.

  ---

  ## 2. Issues by severity

  "Confirmed defect" means behaviour contradicts the docs, the brief or a WCAG
  AA criterion, and I reproduced it. "Design risk / opinion" means the behaviour
  matches the docs, but I think it is risky or wrong.

  ### Critical
  None.

  ### High

  **QA-1: Every story is marked "Not implemented", although the app implements
  them** (confirmed doc defect)
  - **Where:** `docs/user-stories.md` (summary table and every story heading),
  and the README "Status" line.
  - **Steps:** Read the docs, then run the app.
  - **Expected:** Per the brief, each story is marked Implemented or Not
  implemented "based on what the app does today".
  - **Actual:** All nine MVP stories (S-1 to S-8, S-12) say "Not implemented".
  My tests exercise every MVP acceptance criterion, and all pass except the edge
  case in QA-4, which no AC covers.
  - **Evidence:** the passing QA specs listed in section 4.
  - **Fix:** After the planned hand check, mark S-1 to S-8 and S-12 Implemented.
  Keep S-9 to S-11 Not implemented. Update the README status line to match.

  **QA-2: The README's Node minimum (22.12) is wrong; `npm test` fails on 22.12
  to 22.14** (confirmed doc defect)
  - **Where:** README "Prerequisites", from a clean clone.
  - **Steps:** `git clone` → `npm install` → run Vitest under Node 22.12.0 (`npx
  -y node@22.12.0 node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs run`).
  - **Expected:** The tests run, as the README says 22.12 or later is enough.
  - **Actual:** No tests run. Vitest reports 4 unhandled errors: `Failed to
  start forks worker … TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading
  'bind') at
  node_modules/jsdom/node_modules/undici/lib/interceptor/decompress.js:78`.
    - Same failure on 22.13.0 and 22.14.0. Passes on 22.15.0, 22.16, 22.18 to
  22.21 and 22.22.1.
    - jsdom 30.1.3 (and three of its dependencies) declares `node: ^22.22.2 ||
  ^24.15.0 || >=26.0.0`. So `npm install` prints EBADENGINE warnings even on the
  developer's own 22.22.1.
    - Dev, build and preview do work on 22.12.
  - **Evidence:** `scratch/clean-run.log`
  - **Fix:** Either state "Node 22.22.2+ or 24.15+" (matching jsdom's declared
  range, which also removes the warnings), or pin jsdom to a version that
  supports 22.12. Add `"engines"` to package.json.

  **QA-3: The README links `transcripts/`, which does not exist** (confirmed doc
  defect, submission completeness)
  - **Where:** README "AI tool and model", and the repo root (also missing from
  a fresh clone).
  - **Expected:** The transcripts exist (a required deliverable in the brief).
  - **Actual:** Broken link, no folder.
  - **Fix:** Add the exported transcripts before submitting.

  ### Medium

  **QA-4: Some 1-month loans show an EMI 1 cent too low and a spurious "Final
  payment"** (confirmed defect: a wrong result that contradicts README rounding
  rules)
  - **Where:** All themes, viewports and engines. The cause is in
  `src/domain/loan.ts`.
  - **Steps:** Enter $1,000, 0.99%, 1 month.
  - **Expected** (README: "EMI rounded to the nearest cent (halves round up)",
  "Final payment … shown only when it differs from the EMI"): a single payment
  of $1,000.83 (exact value 1,000.825), with no Final payment line.
  - **Actual:** "Monthly payment (EMI) $1,000.82" plus "Final payment
  $1,000.83". That is two different payment figures for a loan with one payment.
  - **How often:** An exhaustive sweep of 1-month loans (8 amounts × every rate
  from 0.01% to 50.00%) found 620 of 40,000 wrong (1.55%). Examples: $1,000 at
  0.09%, 0.21%, 0.33% … 1.35%.
    - Multi-month loans had 0 mismatches in 41,950 sampled cases.
    - Root cause: the EMI comes from floating-point `P·r·g/(g−1)`, which lands
  just below an exact half cent. The code's own `ponytail:` comment acknowledges
  this ceiling, but the README still claims every amount is exact to the cent.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Tests `qa-calculations.spec.ts › figures match the independent reference ›
  one month at 0.99% …` and `… one month at 0.09% on 1,000 …`.
    - Scripts `scratch/half-cent.mjs` and `scratch/compare.mjs`.
  - **Fix:** For `months === 1`, set the EMI to `amount + interestOn(amount)`.
  More generally, compute the EMI with exact integer or rational arithmetic, or
  apply a tiny tolerance before rounding.

  **QA-5: Comma-decimal input is accepted silently as a 10× or 100× value**
  (design risk: matches the README, but conflicts with the brief's "never a
  wrong result")
  - **Where:** All boxes, all engines.
  - **Steps:**
    - Type rate `4,5` → the figures are for 45% (EMI $10,501.23 on $100,000 over
  12 months; 4.5% would be $8,537.85). No message.
    - Amount `1000,50` → $100,050.00. Length `1,2` → 12 months.
    - `12,5` gets a range message, but only by luck.
  - **Why it matters:**
    - The README treats commas as thousands separators and strips them anywhere.
    - Users in comma-decimal locales write 4,5. The docs already consider German
  users for display (S-1.10).
    - On iOS in such regions, the `inputmode="decimal"` keypad offers "," as its
  decimal key. This is general iOS behaviour; I did not verify it on a device.
  - **Evidence:** Tests `qa-calculations.spec.ts › documented comma rule …`.
  They pass, recording the current behaviour.
  - **Fix:** Accept commas only in valid groupings (`1,000` or `1,00,000`).
  Reject a trailing comma group of 1 to 2 digits with a message like "Use a
  point (.) for decimals."

  **QA-6: Comparison figures overprint each other at 1024px for large loans**
  (confirmed layout defect)
  - **Where:** Desktop project at 1024×768 (the `lg` breakpoint), Chromium and
  WebKit, both themes.
  - **Steps:** Three offers at $100,000,000 (50%/120 with 10% fee, 49.99% with
  9.99% fee, 1%/1 month). Also reproduced with three offers of $10M or more on
  the B1 to B3 terms, and with two $5M offers with fixed fees.
  - **Expected:** All figures readable, nothing clipped.
  - **Actual:**
    - EMI and Total cost overlap (e.g. "$4,197,969.76" over "$413,756,375.72").
    - Breakdown values collide.
    - Each "Offer N results" block is 24 to 57px wider than the panel, which has
  `overflow:hidden`, so text is cut off at the right.
    - $2.5M or less was fine at 1024px. 1100px and wider showed no collisions
  for these terms.
    - The B examples fit at 1024px, but tightly: figures spill into the gap
  between columns.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Test `qa-layout.spec.ts › … › 1024px … › largest-values-x3` and `…
  fixed-fee-x2`.
    - Screenshots
  `scratch/evidence/layout-desktop-light-1024-largest-values-x3.png`,
  `…-dark-…`, and `scratch/shots/three-big-1024-light.png`.
  - **Fix:** Use the `fit()` sizing already applied in single-offer mode for the
  comparison figures too. Or switch the row to one column (and the breakdown to
  two columns) when the panel is narrow (container query).

  **QA-7: A box in its error state shows no focus indicator** (confirmed
  accessibility defect, WCAG 2.4.7)
  - **Where:** Both themes; Chromium (desktop and mobile) and WebKit.
  - **Steps:** Make a box invalid and leave it (e.g. rate 60), then Tab back
  into it.
  - **Expected:** A visible change on focus, like every other box gets.
  - **Actual:**
    - The red 2px ring, background and colours are identical focused and
  unfocused. Only the text caret shows where focus is.
    - Cause: the error branch of `InlineBox` in OfferEditor.tsx has no
  `focus-within:` styles, and the input has `outline-none`.
    - All other controls (theme switch, valid boxes, selects, Remove, Add) pass.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Test `qa-a11y.spec.ts › … › every control shows a visible change when
  focused by keyboard, including a box in error`. It compares computed outline,
  box-shadow, background and border; only "rate box in error" fails.
    - In a pixel check, the largest difference was 24 levels of anti-aliasing
  noise.
  - **Fix:** Add `focus-within:` styles to the error state, e.g. a thicker ring
  or an outline with an offset.

  **QA-8: Sideways scrolling at 320 CSS px with realistic fixed fees**
  (confirmed defect against WCAG 1.4.10 Reflow; affects small phones and 1280px
  at 400% zoom)
  - **Where:** Mobile project at 320px, Chromium and iPhone WebKit, both themes.
  - **Steps:** $250,000 / 12.5% / 36 months with a fixed fee of `12,500.50`.
  - **Expected:** No sideways scroll.
  - **Actual:**
    - The page is 16px wider than the screen. The cards, Results panel and Add
  button run past the right edge, while the header stops at the viewport edge.
    - Two $5M offers with fixed fees: 26 to 27px wider.
    - The largest % values ($100,000,000.00 / 49.99 / 120 / 9.99): 1px wider.
    - Cause: the `whitespace-nowrap` clause "[fee box] [fee type]." plus grid
  items sized to their min-content width.
    - Fine at 360px and wider, except a 1px overflow with a $9,999,999.99 fee at
  360px. S-1.11 (375px) passes.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Tests `qa-layout.spec.ts › … › 320px … › fixed-fee-12500.50` (also
  `fixed-fee-x2`, `largest-values-*`).
    - Screenshot
  `scratch/evidence/layout-mobile-light-320-fixed-fee-12500.50.png`.
  - **Fix:** Add `min-w-0` to the grid items and allow the fee clause to wrap
  below about 360px.

  ### Low

  **QA-9: A very long typed or pasted value breaks the layout at every width**
  (confirmed defect, edge case)
  - **Steps:** Paste a 60-digit amount.
  - **Expected:** A message, and the layout intact.
  - **Actual:**
    - The message is correct, but the box grows with no limit (`width: Nch`).
    - Phones (412/375/360/320) and tablet/zoom widths (768/720/640): the page
  becomes about 715 to 837px wide.
    - 1024/1280/1440: the offer card grows under the Results panel and overlaps
  it.
  - **Evidence:**
    - Tests `qa-layout.spec.ts › … › very-long-input` at every width.
    - Screenshots `scratch/evidence/layout-mobile-dark-412-very-long-input.png`
  and `scratch/shots/crop-1440-long.png`.
  - **Fix:** Cap the input width (e.g. `max-width: 100%` or 16ch) and let the
  text scroll inside the box.

  **QA-10: Placeholder text contrast is 2.25:1 (light) and 2.58:1 (dark)**
  (confirmed against WCAG 1.4.3 as W3C applies it to placeholders)
  - **Cause:** `placeholder:text-muted/50`.
  - **Doc conflict:** The comment in `src/index.css` claims "Text pairs meet
  WCAG AA in both themes".
  - **Evidence:** Test `qa-a11y.spec.ts › … › placeholder text … 4.5:1`.
  - **Fix:** Drop the 50% opacity. All other text I measured is between 4.87:1
  and 8.23:1.

  **QA-11: Light-theme text boxes have no visible boundary** (confirmed against
  WCAG 1.4.11 non-text contrast)
  - **Actual:** The box fill (#eef0fa) against the white card is 1.14:1, with no
  border or ring.
  - **Comparison:** The dark theme deliberately adds a 3.1:1 edge
  (`--field-edge`); the light theme does not.
  - **Evidence:** Test `qa-a11y.spec.ts › light theme › each text box is
  distinguishable from its card`.
  - **Fix:** Give the light theme a 1px ring of at least 3:1.

  **QA-12: The README's port instruction is inaccurate** (doc mismatch)
  - **README says:** "If that port is taken, run `npm run dev -- --port 5180`."
  - **Actual:**
    - Vite moves to the next free port by itself and prints it. On this machine
  it printed 5175.
    - With `--port 5180` taken too, it moved to 5181.
    - Harmless, but the instruction implies a failure that doesn't happen.
  - **Evidence:** `scratch/clean-run.log`

  ### Info / suggestions (opinions, not defects)

  - **QA-13:** Error messages are tied to their box with `aria-describedby`, but
  they are not announced when they appear on blur (no live region or
  `role="alert"`). Screen reader users only hear them on returning to the box.
  Suggest `aria-live="polite"` on the message list.
  - **QA-14:** The live region only announces the comparison outcome. With one
  offer it says only "Add another offer to compare.", so the EMI is never
  announced. Suggest announcing "Monthly payment $X, total cost $Y."
  - **QA-15:** Messages don't name their box. Amount `abc` plus fee `abc`
  produces two identical lines, "Enter a number using only digits, a decimal
  point and commas." (see `scratch/evidence/errors-desktop-dark.png`). Suggest
  prefixing the box name.
  - **QA-16:** Two pieces of wording are documented exactly but read oddly:
    - "Add another offer to compare." appears even when a second offer exists
  but is empty or invalid.
    - "Enter the amount borrowed, interest rate and loan length to see results."
  appears under an offer where all three are entered but one is out of range.
  - **QA-17:** The parser is more lenient than the README says ("commas as
  thousands separators"):
    - `1,,000`, `,1000,` and `1,0,0,0` are all read as 1000.
    - `-0` is accepted as a 0% rate.
    - `-$5000` gets the number message rather than the range message the README
  promises for negatives.
  - **QA-18:** The README says Playwright runs `e2e/*.spec.ts`, but `testDir:
  'e2e'` is recursive.
    - `npm run test:e2e` now also runs my `e2e/qa/` specs (46 expected failures
  until the bugs are fixed).
    - It also runs the new untracked `e2e/acceptance.spec.ts`, which appeared
  during my audit (with `e2e/acceptance.config.ts`, `e2e/acceptance-report.mjs`
  and `docs/acceptance-report.md`). These aren't mine and I didn't touch them.
    - Decide whether to ship `e2e/qa/` or add `testIgnore`.
  - **QA-19:** The fee limits are inconsistent: a % fee is capped at 10%, but a
  fixed fee may be up to the amount − $0.01 (99.99%). So a 12% fee can be
  entered as fixed but not as %.

  ---

  ## 3. Requirements and docs challenges

  **Doc-vs-behaviour mismatches:**
  1. Story statuses (QA-1).
  2. Node minimum (QA-2).
  3. `transcripts/` link (QA-3).
  4. "Exact to the cent / halves round up / final payment only when different"
  vs 1-month loans (QA-4).
  5. The comma rule wording vs strip-anywhere behaviour (QA-5, QA-17).
  6. The port instruction (QA-12).
  7. The e2e glob (QA-18).
  8. The index.css "AA" comment vs placeholder contrast (QA-10).

  The claim "for typical offers (10–36%, 12–60 months) the final payment is
  within $1.00 of the EMI" **holds**: over 20,000 random typical offers the
  largest gap was $0.80.

  **Assumptions I would challenge:**
  - **$1,000 minimum.** Defensible as a product decision, but small-dollar
  personal loans ($300 to $1,000) are common for this audience. Fine as long as
  it stays a documented assumption.
  - **$100,000,000 maximum.** Unrealistic for personal loans, and the only
  reason QA-6, most of QA-8 and S-1.6's size requirement exist. A cap of $1M to
  $10M would fit the stated user and remove most of the layout risk.
  - **50% rate cap.** Excludes real subprime offers. Fine for scope, but say so.
  - **"Rounding follows lender practice."** Asserted without a source. Many
  lenders round the EMI up or to whole units. The disclaimer covers this.
  - **Different amounts are all-or-nothing.** A 1-cent difference in one of
  three offers removes every label, including between the two offers that match.
  Consider ranking the matching subset and naming the offer that differs. APR
  (S-11) is the real answer.
  - **"Lowest total cost" is nominal.** It ignores timing across different loan
  lengths. That is acceptable for a non-expert audience and the label is
  factual, but worth a line in the README.
  - **The fee is assumed to be deducted at payout.** A fee financed into the
  loan changes the EMI. Worth stating explicitly.
  - **Accessibility.** The README makes no WCAG claim. Automated scans are
  clean, but manual checks found QA-7, QA-8, QA-10, QA-11 and QA-13.

  **The developer's existing tests** are weaker than they look:
  - The 44px touch-target test checks height only.
  - The sideways-scroll test checks 375px with B1 only.
  - No test checks 1024px, 320px, very long inputs, half-cent rounding or comma
  decimals.

  ---

  ## 4. What I tested that passed

  **Calculation cross-check**

  I built an independent reference in Python (`decimal` at 80 digits) from the
  README rules: `scratch/ref.py`. It has a self-test against A, the largest
  values, and 0%/120 months.
  - My first version had a rounding flaw: dividing the rate first made exact
  half cents round down. I found and fixed it before trusting any comparison.

  Domain code vs reference (`scratch/compare.mjs`):
  - 41,950 cases: a grid of 10 amounts × 13 rates × 15 lengths, plus 20,000
  random offers with % or fixed fees, plus 20,000 "typical" offers.
  - 1 mismatch, which is QA-4.
  - 0 failures of the add-up identities: EMI×(n−1)+final = total repaid, total
  repaid − borrowed = interest, interest + fee = total cost.

  Every figure quoted in the docs, re-derived independently, matches
  (`scratch/doc-figures.py`):
  - A, B1, B2, B3
  - S-1.3, S-1.7, S-2.6, S-3.2 to S-3.8
  - S-4.1, S-4.2, S-5.2, S-5.3, S-7.1, S-7.2, S-12.4

  UI vs reference: 19 cases (`e2e/qa/qa-reference.ts`), checking every displayed
  figure in Chromium (desktop and mobile) and WebKit (desktop and iPhone). 17
  pass; the 2 half-cent cases fail (QA-4). They cover:
  - 0% loans: 3 and 120 months, $100M with a 10% fee, odd cents over 7 months
  - 1-month loans, including the half-cent cases
  - the largest values (% fee, and fixed fee in years)
  - the smallest amount at 50%
  - 0.01% with a 0.01% fee
  - a % fee that is an exact half cent, which correctly rounds up
  - Indian grouping `5,00,000`, and years vs months

  Also passing:
  - Displayed figures add up for three further loan shapes.
  - Switching months/years and %/fixed keeps the number typed and recalculates.
  - Eight amount formats from the README are read correctly.
  - Display stays `$8,884.88` in de-DE, fr-FR, hi-IN and ar-EG.

  **Comparisons** (all pass)
  - S-4.1 / S-5.1 in both themes.
  - Partial tie (S-4.3), full tie including the "Offers 1, 2 and 3 tie …"
  summary (S-4.4), EMI-only tie (S-5.3), S-4.2.
  - Different amounts: two offers, three offers with one differing by a cent,
  the same amount typed three different ways, and changing back (S-7.3).
  - An invalid offer dropping out and returning (S-7.2); an invalid offer
  between two valid ones, which keep their own numbers; S-4.6 / S-7.4.
  - Adding: amount copied from the last offer, cursor placement, an invalid
  amount not copied, an empty offer, a valid amount copied even when another box
  is invalid, the 3-offer limit and the button returning (S-12.1 to S-12.3).
  - Removing: renumbering and re-ranking, the cursor going to Add, a showing
  message staying with its offer, rapid add/remove (S-12.4, S-12.5).
  - Bars drawn to one scale (S-4.9), and the live-region text.

  **Validation** (all pass)
  - All 27 rows of S-6.1. Each checks the message under the offer,
  `aria-invalid`, the `aria-describedby` text, the box turning red, no figures,
  and the placeholder.
  - 33 inputs the docs don't list: `.`, `-`, `$`, `,`, `0.`, `00012`, hex,
  `Infinity`, `NaN`, `+5000`, `$$1000`, spaces and NBSP inside the number,
  full-width and Arabic-Indic digits, 300 digits, 40 decimal places, `$` in
  non-money boxes, `12 %`, `2%`, `12.0` months, tabs, and others.
  - Pasting `250 000`.
  - Switching units while a value is invalid: 6 scenarios.
  - Message timing: S-6.3 to S-6.8, S-3.5, S-3.7 / S-3.8.
  - Rapid key-by-key typing.
  - A 60-step random fuzz. Results were never shown next to a red box, and the
  totals always added up.
  - An automatic check in every QA test found no uncaught error, console error
  or dialog.

  **Flows, theme and privacy** (all pass)
  - First visit in each system theme (S-4.7).
  - A single-offer flow with a correction, and reload clearing everything
  (S-8.3).
  - Comparing then removing.
  - Theme: the switch toggles and is remembered across reloads; the page follows
  the system until a theme is chosen; switching keeps the data; the saved theme
  is applied before the app script runs (no flash); it still works when storage
  throws; a bad saved value falls back to the system theme.
  - Privacy: every request stays on the page's origin, nothing typed is sent or
  stored; it works offline (S-8.2); it never asks for personal details (S-8.4).

  **Accessibility** (pass except QA-7, QA-10, QA-11)
  - axe-core, WCAG 2.0/2.1/2.2 A and AA rules: **0 violations** in 5 states × 2
  themes × 4 browser projects. The 5 states are empty, one offer, messages
  showing, three offers compared, and different amounts.
  - axe best-practice rules: 0 violations.
  - axe left only the highlighted figures' underline-gradient backgrounds
  undecided. I measured those, and the panel and badge text, myself: 4.87:1 to
  8.23:1.
  - S-1.12 Tab order exact in Chromium. In WebKit it is skipped, because Safari
  only Tabs to buttons and selects with a system setting.
  - Keyboard-only add and remove with Enter and Space; focus after add, after
  remove, and after removing from 3 offers.
  - Every control has an accessible name; errors are tied to their box; the live
  region is present (and empty) at load.
  - Touch targets at least 44×44 on phones (width checked as well as height).
  - Reduced motion: no entry animations or bar transitions run.

  **Layout**
  - 1440, 1280, 768, 720 and 640px (the last two stand in for 200% zoom): clean
  in both themes for B1 to B3, the largest values (one offer and three), and
  fixed fees. The only exception is the long input (QA-9).

      295 +/** The value once it has stopped changing for a moment, so ty

      296 +function useSettled(value: string, delay = 600) {             
      297 +  const [settled, setSettled] = useState(value)               
      298 +  useEffect(() => {                                           
      299 +    const timer = setTimeout(() => setSettled(value), delay)  
      300 +    return () => clearTimeout(timer)                          
      301 +  }, [value, delay])                                          
      302 +  return settled                                              
      303 +}

           length * 0.62).toFixed(2)}))`, lineHeight: 1 }

      1  # Loan & EMI Calculator
      2  
      3 -A browser-based calculator for people choosing a personal loan. 
        -Start with one offer and see the monthly payment (EMI), the cash
        - you actually receive after the upfront fee, the total cost of b
        -orrowing, and where every repaid dollar goes. Add up to two more
        - offers to compare them: the lowest total cost and the lowest mo
        -nthly payment are clearly labelled, and every offer is drawn to 
        -the same scale.                                                 
      3 +A browser-based calculator for people choosing a personal loan. 
        +Start with one offer and see the monthly payment (EMI), the cash
        + you actually receive after the upfront fee, the total cost of b
        +orrowing, and what your total repayment is made of. Add up to tw
        +o more offers to compare them: the lowest total cost and the low
        +est monthly payment are clearly labelled, and every offer is dra
        +wn to the same scale.                                           
      4

        -Chromium, desktop and phone sizes). Stories stay marked Not impl
        -emented until each one has also been checked by hand in a real b
        -rowser.                                                         
      5 +> **Status:** the MVP is complete. All nine MVP stories are mark
        +ed Implemented because every one of their acceptance criteria pa
        +sses in real Google Chrome and in Chromium, at desktop and phone
        + sizes, in both themes: see the [acceptance report](docs/accepta
        +nce-report.md). S-9 to S-11 are planned for later and not implem
        +ented. An independent QA audit also ran (see [Tests](#tests)).

         -ing, and a bar showing how the total repaid splits into the fee
         -, the interest and the cash received.

      52  | Term | Meaning |
      53  |---|---|
      54  | Amount borrowed | The loan amount the lender approves. The EM
          I and interest are based on this. |
      55 -| Upfront fee | The processing fee, deducted from the loan befo
         -re it is paid out |                                            
      55 +| Upfront fee | The processing fee, kept by the lender from the
         + loan before it is paid out |                                  
      56  | Amount received | Amount borrowed minus upfront fee: the cash
           that actually reaches the borrower |
      57  | Monthly payment (EMI) | The fixed payment due every month, ex
          cept possibly the last |
      58  | Final payment | The last payment, adjusted by a small amount 
          so the loan ends at exactly zero. Shown only when it differs fr
          om the EMI. |
     ...
      66  
      67  - Fixed interest rate for the whole loan, charged on the reduci
          ng balance, with one payment a month.
      68  - EMI = P × r × (1 + r)^n / ((1 + r)^n − 1), where P is the amo
          unt borrowed, r is the annual rate ÷ 12 ÷ 100, and n is the num
          ber of months. At 0% interest, EMI = P ÷ n.
      69 -- **The upfront fee does not change the EMI.** The EMI is alway
         -s calculated on the full amount borrowed, not the amount receiv
         -ed.                                                            
      70 -- **Rounding follows lender practice.** The EMI is rounded to t
         -he nearest cent (halves round up). Each month's interest is cha
         -rged on the remaining balance and rounded to the nearest cent. 
         -The final payment is whatever clears the balance exactly, so it
         - can differ slightly from the EMI.                             
      69 +- **The upfront fee does not change the EMI.** The fee is kept 
         +from the loan when it is paid out, so the EMI is always calcula
         +ted on the full amount borrowed, not the amount received. If a 
         +lender adds its fee to the loan instead, enter the larger amoun
         +t as the amount borrowed and leave the fee empty.              
      70 +- **Rounding follows lender practice.** The EMI is rounded to t
         +he nearest cent (halves round up), worked out exactly in whole 
         +numbers so floating point can never tip a half cent the wrong w
         +ay. Each month's interest is charged on the remaining balance a
         +nd rounded to the nearest cent. The final payment is whatever c
         +lears the balance exactly, so it can differ slightly from the E
         +MI.                                                            
      71  - Every amount shown is exact to the cent, and the figures alwa
          ys add up: total repaid = EMI × (months − 1) + final payment, a
          nd total interest = total repaid − amount borrowed. At 0% inter
          est, total interest is exactly $0.00.
      72  - We do not use total repaid = EMI × months. With a rounded EMI
          , that formula overstates or understates the real total and bre
          aks at the edges. For example, borrowing $100 at 0% over 3 mont
          hs gives an EMI of $33.33, and $33.33 × 3 = $99.99, which shows
           −$0.01 interest on an interest-free loan.
      73  - A percentage fee is calculated on the amount borrowed and rou
          nded to the nearest cent.
      74  - Lenders round in different ways (some to whole dollars), so t
          heir figures may differ from ours by a small amount. For typica
          l offers (10–36% interest, 12–60 months) the final payment is w
          ithin $1.00 of the EMI.
      75 +- These rules were checked against an independent decimal refer
         +ence calculator in about 42,000 cases, with no differences afte
         +r the one-month rounding fix (see [Tests](#tests)).            
      76  
      77  ### Inputs
      78  
     ...
       85  | Upfront fee (fixed amount) | $0 or more and less than the am
           ount borrowed, up to 2 decimal places |
       86  
       87  - **Product decision:** the minimum amount borrowed is $1,000.
            Personal loans smaller than that are rare, and the app is for
            people comparing real offers.
       87 -- Plain numbers only: an optional minus sign, digits, one opti
          -onal decimal point, and optional commas as thousands separator
          -s. Commas and spaces at either end are ignored, so both `500,0
          -00` and `5,00,000` work. The money boxes show a `$` beside the
          -m, and a `$` typed in front of the number there is ignored too
          -. Anything else, such as letters, `%` or `1e5`, is rejected wi
          -th a message. A negative number gets the box's range message. 
       88 +- **Product decision:** a fixed fee only has to be less than t
          +he amount borrowed, while a percentage fee is capped at 10%. F
          +ixed fees are quoted as flat sums rather than as a share of th
          +e loan, so they are not held to the percentage cap.           
       89 +- Plain numbers only: an optional minus sign, digits and one o
          +ptional decimal point. Commas are allowed only between groups 
          +of digits, in Western (`250,000`) or Indian (`2,50,000`) group
          +ing. A comma anywhere else, such as a decimal comma in `4,5`, 
          +gets the message "Use a point for decimals (4.5) and commas on
          +ly between thousands (250,000).", so it can never be misread a
          +s a larger number. Spaces at either end are ignored. The money
          + boxes show a `$` beside them, and a `$` typed in front of the
          + number there is ignored too. Anything else, such as letters, 
          +`%` or `1e5`, is rejected with a message. A negative number ge
          +ts the box's range message.                                   
       90  - Amount borrowed, interest rate and loan length are required.
            The upfront fee is optional: an empty fee counts as $0.
       91  - Loan length starts in months and the upfront fee starts as a
            percentage. Switching to years, or to a fixed amount, keeps t
           he number typed and changes only what it means.
       90 -- Messages appear when the user leaves a box. At that moment, 
          -every box in the offer that the user has visited and that is i
          -nvalid shows its message, so lowering the amount borrowed belo
          -w a fixed fee reveals the fee message (S-3.7). Changing months
          -/years or %/fixed counts as leaving that box. A message that i
          -s showing updates as the user types and disappears as soon as 
          -the value is valid. An offer's figures are hidden the moment a
          -ny of its boxes is missing or invalid, even before a message a
          -ppears.                                                       
       91 -- Each offer is validated on its own. An offer with every box 
          -empty counts as not entered: it shows no messages and no resul
          -ts. An offer with any missing or invalid box shows its message
          -s and no results, and is left out of the comparison; the other
          - offers still work.                                           
       92 +- Messages appear when the user leaves a box. At that moment, 
          +every box in the offer that the user has visited and that is i
          +nvalid shows its message, so lowering the amount borrowed belo
          +w a fixed fee reveals the fee message (S-3.7). Changing months
          +/years or %/fixed counts as leaving that box. A message that i
          +s showing updates as the user types and disappears as soon as 
          +the value is valid. Each message starts with its box's name. A
          +n offer's figures are hidden the moment any of its boxes is mi

       95  ### Display
       96  
       97  - Every amount is shown in dollars, as `$1,234,567.89`, whatev
           er the browser's language. The `$` is a display choice only: n
           othing is converted, and the maths is the same in any currency
           .
       98  - **Light and dark themes.** The first visit follows the syste
           m setting, and keeps following it until the user picks a theme
            with the switch in the header. The choice is remembered in th
           is browser. The theme is applied before the page draws, so it 
           never flashes the wrong colours.
       99 +- The two choices in each offer (months/years and % of amount/
          +fixed amount) are dropdowns drawn by the app, so they look the
          + same in both themes and every browser instead of opening the 
          +operating system's menu.                                      
      100  - The themes are a display preference rather than one of the n
           eeds in [Jobs To Be Done](docs/jobs-to-be-done.md), so they ha
           ve no user story; the end-to-end tests cover them.
      101  
      102  ### Offers and comparison
     ...
      105  - Any offer can be removed while there are at least two. Offer
           s are numbered by position, so removing Offer 2 makes Offer 3 
           the new Offer 2. After a removal the cursor goes to **Add anot
           her offer**.
      106  - Only valid offers are compared, and labels and differences u
           pdate as soon as any offer changes.
      107  - **No valid offers:** no figures and no labels.
      105 -- **One valid offer:** its results are shown in full with the 
          -note "Add another offer to compare." There are no labels.     
      108 +- **One valid offer:** its results are shown in full, with no 
          +labels. The note says "Add another offer to compare." when it 
          +is the only offer, or "Complete another offer to compare." whe
          +n another offer is on the page but empty or invalid.          
      109  - **Different amounts borrowed:** offers for different amounts
            cannot be ranked fairly. If the valid offers are not all for 
           the same amount borrowed, each offer's results are still shown
           , but there are no labels or differences, and a message asks f
           or the same amount borrowed in every offer. Loan lengths and f
           ees may differ; showing that trade-off is the point.
      110  - **Labels:** "Lowest total cost" goes to the offer with the l
           owest total cost of borrowing. "Lowest monthly payment" goes t
           o the offer with the lowest EMI (not the final payment). One o
           ffer can carry both. A summary states the outcome, for example
            "Offer 1 has the lowest total cost: $53,582.58."
      111 +- **Total cost is in plain dollars.** It adds up what each loa
          +n costs over its whole life and does not adjust for when the m
          +oney is paid, so a longer loan's later payments are not discou
          +nted. That keeps the figure checkable by hand; APR (S-11) is t
          +he planned answer for a time-adjusted comparison.             
      112  - **Ranking uses the amounts shown**, rounded to the cent, so 
           a label never contradicts the figures on screen.
      113  - **Ties:** every offer that shares the lowest value gets the 
           label with "(tied)" added, for example "Lowest total cost (tie
           d)", and the summary says the offers tie. If all valid offers 
           tie, they all get it.
      114  - **Differences:** each offer without the "Lowest total cost" 
           label shows how much more its total cost of borrowing is than 
           the lowest, for example "Costs $1,250.00 more in total". Offer
           s tied for lowest show no difference.
      111 -- **Repayment bar:** each offer's total repaid is drawn as a b
          -ar split into the upfront fee, the total interest and the amou
          -nt received, with the cost parts first. When comparing, every 
          -bar uses the same scale, so the cost parts can be compared by 
          -length.                                                       
      115 +- **What you repay:** each offer's total repaid is drawn as a 
          +bar made of the amount received plus the cost of borrowing (up
          +front fee and interest), with the cost parts first, and a sent
          +ence spells it out, for example "You repay $106,618.53 in tota
          +l: the $98,000.00 you receive, plus $8,618.53 cost of borrowin
          +g (the upfront fee and the interest)." The fee is part of the 
          +total repaid because it was kept from the loan at payout and i
          +s repaid within the amount borrowed; it is not added to any mo
          +nthly payment. When comparing, every bar uses the same scale, 
          +so the cost parts can be compared by length.                  
      116  
      117  ### Privacy and advice
      118  
      119  - All calculations run in the browser. The page loads its own 
           files, including its font, but nothing the user enters is ever
            sent anywhere or stored, and refreshing the page clears it. T
           he only thing saved is the theme choice (`theme` in the browse
           r's local storage).
      120  - The app never recommends an offer. A short note says results
            are estimates.
      121  
      122 +### Accessibility                                             
      123 +                                                              
      124 +- Everything works with the keyboard alone, in a logical order
          +, with a visible focus outline on every control, including a b
          +ox that is showing an error.                                  
      125 +- Every box and dropdown has a name for screen readers. Error 
          +messages are tied to their box and announced as they appear, a
          +nd the results are announced once typing pauses.              
      126 +- Text meets WCAG AA contrast in both themes, including placeh
          +olders, and every box has an edge with at least 3:1 contrast. 
      127 +- On phones, every box, dropdown and button is at least 44 pix
          +els tall, and nothing scrolls sideways down to 320 pixels wide
          + (which also covers zooming in to 400% on a desktop).         
      128 +- Animations are switched off when the system asks for reduced
          + motion.                                                      
      129 +- Checked with automated axe-core scans and Playwright tests. 
          +It has not been tried with a real screen reader by hand.      
      130 +

          - the results. All user-facing messages are in `messages.ts`.

          -ntence with the boxes filled in, plus the rule for when messag
          -es appear.                                                    
      174 -- `src/components/Results.tsx`: the results panel, the repayme
          -nt bar and the comparison.

          +er-facing messages are in `messages.ts`.

    It wasn't confirmed or fixed.
  - The QA agent's own tests in e2e/qa/ were left half-edited and haven't been
    run since. npm run build type-checks those files, and the build hasn't been
    re-run since those edits. Run npm run build once before submitting.
  - The acceptance report can't be regenerated without a fresh run, because a
    later test run deleted the raw results file it was built from.
  - The bundle grew from about 85 KB to about 116 KB gzipped because of the
    Radix dropdowns.

  For submission
  1. Export the session transcripts with /export into a transcripts/ folder. The
     README links to it, but it doesn't exist yet.
  2. Rename the project folder to firstname-lastname-calculator. Leave out the
     assignment PDF (it's already in .gitignore) and node_modules.
  3. Share it as a GitHub or Drive link and email it with the subject "Take-home
     assignment: [role], [full name]".
